a_{j,2}^{L}(\tilde{A}_{j,2},\overline{\tilde{A}_{j,2}})=\left(\alpha_{1}A_{1,2}^{2}+\alpha_{2}|A_{1,2}|^{2}+\alpha_{3}\overline{A_{1,2}}^{2}\right)\tilde{A}_{j,2}+\left(\beta_{1}A_{1,2}^{2}+\beta_{2}|A_{1,2}|^{2}+\beta_{3}\overline{A_{1,2}}^{2}\right)\overline{\tilde{A}_{j,2}},

and note that
a_{j,2}^{N}
depends only modulation functions
A_{m^{\prime}j^{\prime},2}
at lower orders with
\alpha(m^{\prime})+j^{\prime}<j
.

We prove the following result.

Lemma 5.16
.

Let
\theta_{A}=\theta_{A}(j)=3(j-1)+\theta
where
j\in\{3,\ldots,N-2\}
and
\theta>1/2
, and assume that
a_{j,2}^{N}(\cdot,t_{2})\in H_{ul}^{\theta}
for all
t_{2}\in[0,T_{2}]
. Then
A_{j,2}(\cdot,t_{2})\in H_{ul}^{\theta}
for all
t_{2}\in[0,T_{2}]
. If additionally
\mu(x)\equiv 0
and

\|a_{j,2}^{N}(\cdot,t_{2})\|_{H_{ul}^{\theta}}\leq C\max_{k=1,\ldots,j-1}\|A_{k,2}(\cdot,t_{2})\|_{H_{ul}^{\theta_{k,j}}}^{3},

(109)

then for
K>0
sufficiently small it holds that

\|A_{j,2}(\cdot,T_{2})\|_{H_{ul}^{\theta}}\leq C\max_{k=1,\ldots,j}\|A_{k,2}^{\ast}\|_{H_{ul}^{\theta_{k,j}}},

(110)

where
\theta_{k,j}
is given by (
84
).

Proof.

We proceed similarly to the proof of Lemma
5.7
in Step IV of the chart
\mathcal{K}_{1}
analysis, and therefore present fewer details. As an induction hypothesis, we assume that
A_{k,2}(\cdot,t_{2})\in H_{ul}^{\theta_{k,j}}
for all
k=3,\ldots,j-1\leq N-3
. We need to show that
A_{j,2}(\cdot,t_{2})\in H_{ul}^{\theta}
. We omit the explicit treatment of the base case again since it is similar to the induction step.

The equation for
A_{j,2}
is given by

\partial_{t_{2}}A_{j,2}=4\partial_{x_{2}}^{2}A_{j,2}+v_{2}(t_{2})A_{j,2}-a_{j,2}-4i\partial_{x_{2}}^{3}A_{j-1,2}+\partial_{x_{2}}^{4}A_{j-2,2},\qquad A_{j,2}(x_{2},0)=A_{j,2}^{\ast}(x_{2}),

and we obtain a new equation

\partial_{t_{2}}\tilde{A}_{j,2}=4\partial_{x_{2}}^{2}\tilde{A}_{j,2}-a_{j,2}^{L}\left(\tilde{A}_{j,2},\overline{\tilde{A}_{j,2}}\right)-\mathrm{e}^{-I_{2}(t_{2})}\left(a_{j,2}^{N}+4i\partial_{x_{2}}^{3}A_{j-1,2}+\partial_{x_{2}}^{4}A_{j-2,2}\right),

with
\tilde{A}_{j,2}(x_{2},0)=A_{j,2}^{\ast}(x_{2})
, after the transformation
A_{j,2}(x_{2},t_{2})=:\mathrm{e}^{I_{2}(t_{2})}\tilde{A}_{j,2}(x_{2},t_{2})
. We now apply the variation of constants formula and split the solution into two parts
\tilde{A}_{j,2}=\tilde{A}_{j,2,h}+\tilde{A}_{j,2,s}
, where

\begin{split}\tilde{A}_{j,2,h}(\cdot,t_{2})&=\mathrm{e}^{t_{2}\Lambda_{2}}A_{j,2}^{\ast}-\int_{0}^{t_{2}}\mathrm{e}^{(t_{2}-s_{2})\Lambda_{2}}a_{j,2}^{L}\left(\tilde{A}_{j,2}(\cdot,s_{2}),\overline{\tilde{A}_{j,2}}(\cdot,s_{2})\right)ds_{2},\\
\tilde{A}_{j,2,s}(\cdot,t_{2})&=-\int_{0}^{t_{2}}\mathrm{e}^{(t_{2}-s_{2})\Lambda_{2}}\mathrm{e}^{-I_{2}(s_{2})}\left(a^{N}_{j,2}(\cdot,s_{2})+4i\partial_{x_{2}}^{3}A_{j-1,2}(\cdot,s_{2})+\partial_{x_{2}}^{4}A_{j-2,2}(\cdot,s_{2})\right)ds_{2},\end{split}

(111)

for
t_{2}\in[0,T_{2}]
.

Using the same bound for the semigroup as in Steps II-III above, as well as the bound for
A_{1,2}
in Lemma
5.14
, we obtain

\|\tilde{A}_{j,2,h}(\cdot,t_{2})\|_{H_{ul}^{\theta}}\leq C\|A_{j,1}^{\ast}\|_{H_{ul}^{\theta}}+C\tilde{T}_{j,2}\|A_{1,2}^{\ast}\|_{H_{ul}^{\theta}}^{2}\leq C\max_{k=1,\ldots,j}\|A_{j,1}^{\ast}\|_{H_{ul}^{\theta}},

(112)

for
t_{2}\in[0,\tilde{T}_{j,2}]
, where
\tilde{T}_{j,2}:=\min\{T_{2},\sup\{t_{2}>0:\|\tilde{A}_{j,2}(\cdot,s_{2})\|_{H_{ul}^{\theta}}\leq\tilde{M}\}\}
.

For the source term
\tilde{A}_{j,2,s}
we have

\begin{split}\|\tilde{A}_{j,2,s}(\cdot,t_{2})\|_{H_{ul}^{\theta}}&\leq C\int_{0}^{t_{2}}\big\|a^{N}_{j,2}(\cdot,s_{2})+4i\partial_{x_{2}}^{3}A_{j-1,2}(\cdot,s_{2})+\partial_{x_{2}}^{4}A_{j-2,2}(\cdot,s_{2})\big\|_{H_{ul}^{\theta}}ds_{2}\\
&\leq C\tilde{T}_{j,2}\left(\big\|a^{N}_{3,2}(\cdot,t_{2})\big\|_{H_{ul}^{\theta}}+\|\partial_{x_{2}}^{3}A_{j-1,2}(\cdot,t_{2})\big\|_{H_{ul}^{\theta}}+\|\partial_{x_{2}}^{4}A_{j-2,2}(\cdot,t_{2})\big\|_{H_{ul}^{\theta}}\right),\end{split}

(113)

for
t_{2}\in[0,\tilde{T}_{j,2}]
. Since
a^{N}_{3,2}(\cdot,t_{2})\in H_{ul}^{\theta}
by assumption and

\begin{split}\|\partial_{x_{2}}^{3}A_{j-1,2}(\cdot,t_{2})\big\|_{H_{ul}^{\theta}}&\leq C\|A_{j-1,2}(\cdot,t_{2})\big\|_{H_{ul}^{\theta_{j-1,j}}}\leq C,\\
\|\partial_{x_{2}}^{4}A_{j-2,2}(\cdot,t_{2})\big\|_{H_{ul}^{\theta}}&\leq C\|A_{j-2,2}(\cdot,t_{2})\big\|_{H_{ul}^{\theta_{j-2,j}}}\leq C,\end{split}

by our regularity assumptions and the induction hypothesis,
\tilde{A}_{j,2,s}(\cdot,t_{2})\in H_{ul}^{\theta}
for all
t_{2}\in[0,\tilde{T}_{j,2}]
.

Since the integrating factor
e^{I_{2}(t_{2})}
is also bounded for all
t_{2}\in[0,\tilde{T}_{2,j}]
, it follows that
A_{j,2}(\cdot,t_{2})=\mathrm{e}^{I_{2}(t_{2})}(\tilde{A}_{j,2,h}(\cdot,t_{2})+\tilde{A}_{j,2,s}(\cdot,t_{2}))\in H_{ul}^{\theta}
for all
t_{2}\in[0,\tilde{T}_{j,2}]
. We may set
\tilde{T}_{j,2}=T_{2}
, because the bounds above are finite for all
t_{2}\in[0,T_{2}]
.

It remains to prove the bound in (
110
). Under the additional assumptions that (
109
) is satisfied and
\mu(x)\equiv 0
we can obtain a better bound on the norm of
\tilde{A}_{j,2,s}
. This is achieved by using (
109
) and

\begin{split}\|\partial_{x_{2}}^{3}A_{j-1,2}(\cdot,t_{2})\big\|_{H_{ul}^{\theta}}&\leq C\|A_{j-1,2}(\cdot,t_{2})\big\|_{H_{ul}^{\theta_{j-1,j}}}\leq C\max_{k=1,\ldots,j-1}\|A_{k,2}^{\ast}\|_{H_{ul}^{\theta_{k,j}}},\\
\|\partial_{x_{2}}^{4}A_{j-2,2}(\cdot,t_{2})\big\|_{H_{ul}^{\theta}}&\leq C\|A_{j-2,2}(\cdot,t_{2})\big\|_{H_{ul}^{\theta_{j-2,j}}}\leq C\max_{k=1,\ldots,j-2}\|A_{k,2}^{\ast}\|_{H_{ul}^{\theta_{k,j}}},\end{split}

where the right-most bounds can be derived using a recursive argument base on the improved bounds from Lemmas
5.15
and
5.16
in case
\mu(x)\equiv 0
. Substituting these bounds into the right-hand side of (
113
), combining the resulting bound with (
112
) and multiplying through by the (bounded) integrating factor yields the result in (
110
).
∎

5.2.1
Step V: Estimate
\psi_{2}

As for the
\mathcal{K}_{1}
analysis in Section
5.1
, we need to control the non-critical modulation functions
A_{mj,2}
with
|m|\neq 1
in order to describe the dynamics of
\psi_{2}(\cdot,T_{2})
and the map
\pi_{2}:\Sigma^{in}_{2}\to\Sigma^{out}_{2}
. This is also necessary in order to verify the assumptions on
a_{j,2}^{N}
in Lemma
5.16
. As before, we reinstate the full notation
A_{\pm 1j,2}
instead of
A_{j,2}
in order to distinguish modulation functions with
|m|=1
and
|m|\neq 1
,
and recall that
\textbf{A}_{mj,2}
denotes the set of lower order critical modulation functions
A_{\pm 1j^{\prime},2}
with
j^{\prime}\in\{1,\ldots,\alpha(m)+j-1\}
.

Lemma 5.17
.

Let
\theta_{A}=1+3(N-3)+\theta
where
\theta>1/2
. Then the conditions on
a_{j,2}^{N}
for the applicability of Lemma
5.16
are satisfied. We have that
A_{\pm 1j,2}(\cdot,t_{2})\in H_{ul}^{\theta_{j,N-2}}
for all
j=1,\ldots,N-2
and
t_{2}\in[0,T_{2}]
, and

A_{mj,2}(\cdot,t_{1})=g_{mj,2}^{gl}\left(\textbf{{A}}_{mj,2}(\cdot,t_{2}),v_{2}(t_{2})\right)\in H_{ul}^{\theta_{\alpha(m)+j,N}},

(114)

for all
t_{2}\in[0,T_{2}]
,
m\in I_{N}\setminus\{-1,1\}
and
j=1,\ldots,\tilde{\alpha}(m)
. If additionally
\mu(x)\equiv 0
then for each
m\in I_{N}\setminus\{-1,1\}
we have

\big\|g_{mj,2}^{gl}\left(\textbf{{A}}_{mj,2}(\cdot,T_{2}),v_{2}(T_{2})\right)\big\|_{H_{ul}^{\theta_{j,N-2}}}\leq C\max_{k=1,\ldots,j-2}\|A_{1k,2}^{\ast}\|_{H_{ul}^{\theta_{k,N-2}}},

(115)

for all
j=1,\ldots,\tilde{\alpha}(m)
.

Proof.

The proof is analogous to the proof of Lemma
5.9
, so we omit the details. The reason that we obtain constant bounds in the case
\mu(x)\equiv 0
(as opposed the the exponential bounds in Lemma
5.9
) is that we have only constant bounds when
\nu_{1}=0
in Lemmas
5.14
,
5.15
and
5.16
.
∎

Combining the results of Lemmas
5.13
,
5.14
,
5.15
,
5.16
and
5.17
we obtain the following result, which summarises the dynamics in chart
\mathcal{K}_{2}
.

Proposition 5.18
.

Let
\theta_{A}=1+3(N-3)+\theta
where
\theta>1/2
. Then for
\vartheta,K>0
sufficiently small but fixed we have
\psi_{2}(\cdot,t_{2})\in H_{ul}^{\theta}
for all
t_{2}\in[0,T_{2}]
. In particular, the map
\pi_{2}:\Sigma_{2}^{in}\to\Sigma_{2}^{out}
is well-defined and given by

\pi_{2}:(\psi_{2}^{\ast},-\zeta^{-1/2},r_{2})\mapsto\left(\psi_{2}(\cdot,T_{2}),\rho_{max},r_{2}\right),

(116)

with

\psi_{2}(x,t_{2})=\psi_{GL,2}(x,t_{2})+r_{2}\left(A_{2,2,h}(x,t_{2})\mathrm{e}^{ix}+c.c.\right)+r_{2}^{2}h_{rem,2}(x,t_{2}),

(117)

where
\psi_{GL,2}(x,t_{2})=A_{1,2}(x_{2},T_{2})\mathrm{e}^{ix}+c.c.
satisfies
\|\psi_{GL,2}(\cdot,t_{2})\|_{H_{ul}^{\theta_{A}-1}}\leq C\|A_{1,2}^{\ast}\|_{H_{ul}^{\theta_{A}}}
, the function
A_{2,2}
is described in Lemma
5.15
, and the remainder term
h_{rem,2}(\cdot,t_{2})\in H_{ul}^{\theta}
for all
t_{2}\in[0,T_{2}]
.
If additionally
\mu(x)\equiv 0
then we have

\|\psi_{2}(\cdot,t_{2})\|_{H_{ul}^{\theta}}\leq C\|A_{1,2}^{\ast}\|_{H_{ul}^{\theta+1}}+r_{2}C\max_{k=1,2}\|A_{k,2}^{\ast}\|_{H_{ul}^{\theta+1}}+r_{2}^{2}C\max_{k=1,\ldots,N-2}\|A_{k,2}^{\ast}\|_{H_{ul}^{\theta+1}},

(118)

for all
t_{2}\in[0,T_{2}]
.

Proof.

The form of the map in (
116
) follows immediately from Lemma
5.13
. The fact that
\psi_{2}(\cdot,t_{2})\in H_{ul}^{\theta}
for all
t_{2}\in[0,T_{2}]
follows from its definition in (
45
) and the fact that
A_{mj,2}(\cdot,t_{2})\in H_{ul}^{\theta+1}
for all
m\in I_{N}
,
j=1,\ldots,\tilde{\alpha}(m)
and
t_{2}\in[0,T_{2}]
due to Lemmas
5.14
,
5.15
,
5.16
and
5.17
. Similarly to the proof of Proposition
5.11
, we need one extra degree of regularity wherever we use Lemma
A.1
to take the
H_{ul}^{\theta}
norm with respect to
x
instead of
x_{2}
.

The asymptotic expression in (
117
) is obtained directly by evaluating the series expansion (
45
) and evaluating it at
t_{2}=T_{2}
. The details are similar to the derivation of the asymptotic formula (
97
) in the proof of Proposition
5.11
, and so omitted here for brevity.

Finally, the bound in (
118
) follows after applying a triangle inequality to the series definitions of
\psi_{2}
and applying the assertions pertaining to the case
\mu(x)\equiv 0
in Lemmas
5.14
,
5.15
,
5.16
and
5.17
.
∎

5.3
Proof of Lemma
3.14

Lemma
3.14
can be proved with the information obtained in charts
\mathcal{K}_{1}
and
\mathcal{K}_{2}
. The idea is to analyse
\pi^{mid}:\Delta^{in}\to\Delta^{mid}
via its pre-image
\pi_{2}\circ\kappa_{12}\circ\pi_{1}:\Sigma_{1}^{in}\to\Sigma_{2}^{out}
in the blown-up space, where
\kappa_{12}
is the change of coordinates map defined in (
46
) and
\pi_{1}
,
\pi_{2}
are the maps described in Propositions
5.11
,
5.18
respectively. The fact that
\Psi(\cdot,t)=r(\bar{t})\psi(\cdot,\bar{t})\in H_{ul}^{\theta}
for all
t\in[0,T_{mid}]
follows directly from the fact that
\psi_{l}(\cdot,t_{l})\in H_{ul}^{\theta}
for all
t_{l}\in[0,T_{l}]
in charts
\mathcal{K}_{l}
and
\mathcal{K}_{2}
; recall Propositions
5.11
and
5.18
. It therefore remains to show that the map
\pi^{mid}
is given by (
38
).

We consider an initial condition
(\Psi^{\ast},-\rho_{in})\in\Delta^{in}
, which corresponds to an initial condition of the form
(\psi_{1}^{\ast},\sqrt{\rho_{in}},\varepsilon_{1}^{\ast})\in\Sigma_{1}^{in}
defined by

\psi_{1}^{\ast}=\frac{\Psi^{\ast}}{\sqrt{\rho_{in}}},\qquad r_{1}^{\ast}=\sqrt{\rho_{in}},\qquad\varepsilon_{1}^{\ast}=\frac{\varepsilon}{\rho_{in}^{2}}.

(119)

Since
\Sigma_{2}^{in}=\kappa_{12}(\Sigma_{1}^{out})
, we obtain the following bounds for the initial modulation functions in chart
\mathcal{K}_{2}
using the change of coordinates formula
A_{mj,2}^{\ast}=\zeta^{-(\alpha(m)+j)/4}A_{mj,1}^{\ast}(\cdot,T_{1})
, recall (
47
), together with Lemmas
5.4
,
5.6
,
5.7
and
5.9
:

\begin{split}&\|A_{1,2}^{\ast}\|_{H_{ul}^{\theta_{A}}}=\zeta^{-1/4}\|A_{1,1}(\cdot,T_{1})\|_{H_{ul}^{\theta_{A}}}\leq C\mathrm{e}^{-\kappa/2\varepsilon_{1}^{\ast}}\|A_{1,1}^{\ast}\|_{H_{ul}^{\theta_{A}}},\\
&\|A_{2,2}^{\ast}\|_{H_{ul}^{\theta_{2,N-2}}}=\zeta^{-1/2}\|A_{2,1}(\cdot,T_{1})\|_{H_{ul}^{\theta_{2,N-2}}}\leq C\left(|\nu_{1}|f_{2,1}(\varepsilon_{1}^{\ast})+\mathrm{e}^{-\kappa/2\varepsilon_{1}^{\ast}}\max_{k=1,2}\|A_{k,1}^{\ast}\|_{H_{ul}^{\theta_{k,N-2}}}\right),\\
&\|A_{j,2}^{\ast}\|_{H_{ul}^{\theta_{j,N-2}}}=\zeta^{-j/4}\|A_{j,1}(\cdot,T_{1})\|_{H_{ul}^{\theta_{j,N-2}}}\leq C,\\
&\|A_{mj,2}^{\ast}\|_{H_{ul}^{\theta_{\alpha(m)+j,N}}}=\zeta^{-(\alpha(m)+j)/4}\|A_{mj,1}(\cdot,T_{1})\|_{H_{ul}^{\theta_{\alpha(m)+j,N}}}\leq C,\end{split}

(120)

where
j\geq 3
in the second-to-last line and
|m|\neq 1
in the last. This provides sufficient information on initial conditions

\left(\psi_{2}^{\ast},-\zeta^{-1/2},r_{2}\right)=\kappa_{12}\left(\psi_{1}(\cdot,T_{1}),\sqrt{\rho_{in}}\left(\frac{\varepsilon_{1}^{\ast}}{\zeta}\right)^{1/4},\varepsilon_{1}^{\ast}\right)=\left(\zeta^{-1/4}\psi_{1}(\cdot,T_{1}),-\zeta^{-1/2},\sqrt{\rho_{in}}{\varepsilon_{1}^{\ast}}^{1/4}\right)

in
\Sigma_{2}^{in}
, now in chart
\mathcal{K}_{2}
coordinates. The evolution from
\Sigma_{2}^{in}
up to
\Sigma_{2}^{out}
is described by Proposition
5.18
. Using Proposition
5.18
and the bound for
\|A_{1,2}^{\ast}\|_{H_{ul}^{\theta_{A}}}
in (
120
) we have that

\|\psi_{GL,2}(\cdot,T_{2})\|_{{H_{ul}^{\theta_{A}-1}}}\leq C\|A_{1,2}^{\ast}\|_{H_{ul}^{\theta_{A}}}\leq C\mathrm{e}^{-\kappa/2\varepsilon_{1}^{\ast}}\|A_{1,1}^{\ast}\|_{H_{ul}^{\theta_{A}}}.

Thus
\psi_{GL,2}(\cdot,T_{2})
can be pushed into the higher order terms in (
117
), such that we obtain

\psi_{2}(x,t_{2})=r_{2}\left(A_{2,2}(x_{2},t_{2})\mathrm{e}^{ix}+c.c.\right)+r_{2}^{2}\tilde{h}_{rem,2}(x,t_{2}),

(121)

where
\tilde{h}_{rem,2}(\cdot,t_{2})\in H_{ul}^{\theta}
for all
t_{2}\in[0,T_{2}]
and

\left\|A_{2,2}(r_{2}\cdot,T_{2})\mathrm{e}^{i\cdot}+c.c.\right\|_{H_{ul}^{\theta_{2,N-2}}}\leq C\left(|\nu_{1}|+\mathrm{e}^{-\kappa/2\varepsilon_{1}^{\ast}}\right),

due to (
120
) and Lemma
5.15
.

In order to express (
121
) in terms of the original function
\Psi
we simply apply the blow-down transformations

\Psi(x,T_{mid})=r_{2}\psi_{2}(x,T_{2})=\varepsilon^{1/4}\psi_{2}\left(x,\sqrt{\rho_{in}}{\varepsilon_{1}^{\ast}}^{1/4}\right)

and
\varepsilon_{1}^{\ast}=\varepsilon/\rho_{in}^{2}
, thereby obtaining

\Psi(x,T_{mid})=\varepsilon^{1/2}\left(A_{2,2}(\varepsilon^{1/4}x,T_{2})\mathrm{e}^{ix}+c.c.\right)+\varepsilon^{3/4}R(x,\varepsilon),

where

\left\|A_{2,2}(\varepsilon^{1/4}\cdot,T_{2})\mathrm{e}^{i\cdot}+c.c.\right\|_{H_{ul}^{\theta}}\leq C\left(|\nu_{1}|+\mathrm{e}^{-\kappa\rho_{in}^{2}/2\varepsilon}\right)

and
R(\cdot,\varepsilon):=\tilde{h}_{rem,2}(\cdot,T_{2})\in H_{ul}^{\theta}
as required. This concludes the proof of Lemma
3.14
.
∎

5.4
Chart
\mathcal{K}_{3}

In order to prove Lemma
3.16
we also need to understand the dynamics in chart
\mathcal{K}_{3}
. We recall the equations in chart
\mathcal{K}_{3}
:

\begin{split}\partial_{t_{3}}A_{1,3}&=4\partial_{x_{3}}^{2}A_{1,3}+\left(1-\frac{\varepsilon_{3}}{2}\right)A_{1,3}-3A_{1,3}|A_{1,3}|^{2},\\
\partial_{t_{3}}r_{3}&=\frac{1}{2}r_{3}\varepsilon_{3},\\
\partial_{t_{3}}\varepsilon_{3}&=-2\varepsilon_{3}^{2},\\
\partial_{t_{3}}A_{2,3}&=4\partial_{x_{3}}^{2}A_{2,3}+\left(1-\frac{\varepsilon_{3}}{2}\right)A_{2,3}-a_{2,3}-4i\partial_{x_{3}}^{3}A_{1,3},\\
\partial_{t_{3}}A_{j,3}&=4\partial_{x_{3}}^{2}A_{j,3}+\left(1-\frac{\varepsilon_{3}}{2}\right)A_{j,3}-a_{j,3}-4i\partial_{x_{3}}^{3}A_{j-1,3}-\partial_{x_{3}}^{4}A_{j-2,3},\end{split}

(122)

for
j=3,\ldots,N-2
. Note that we have set
\nu_{1}=0
in the equation for
A_{2,3}
since our goal is to prove Lemma
3.16
, which has
\mu(x)\equiv 0
. Our aim in chart
\mathcal{K}_{3}
is to describe the map

\pi_{3}:\Sigma^{in}_{3}\ni\left(\psi_{3}^{\ast},r_{3}^{\ast},\zeta\right)\mapsto\left(\psi_{3}(\cdot,T_{3}),\rho_{out}^{1/2},\varepsilon_{3}^{out}(r_{3}^{\ast})\right)\in\Sigma_{3}^{out},

induced by forward evolution of initial conditions in

\Sigma_{3}^{in}=\left\{(\psi_{3}^{\ast},r_{3},\zeta):\|\psi_{3}^{\ast}\|_{H_{ul}^{\theta}}\leq K,r_{3}\in[0,\zeta^{-1/4}\vartheta]\right\},

which corresponds to the exit section
\Sigma^{out}_{2}
in chart
\mathcal{K}_{2}
coordinates via
\Sigma_{3}^{in}=\kappa_{23}(\Sigma_{2}^{out})
if we set
\rho_{mid}=\zeta^{-1/2}
(which we shall assume is the case from this point on), up to the exit section

\Sigma_{3}^{out}=\left\{(\psi_{3}^{\ast},\rho_{out}^{1/2},\varepsilon_{3}):\|\psi_{3}^{\ast}\|_{H_{ul}^{\theta}}\leq K,\varepsilon_{3}\in[0,\zeta]\right\},

which corresponds to the original exit section
\Delta^{out}
defined in (
37
)
after blowing down, fixing
\varepsilon
, and using the natural identification with points in
\Delta^{out}_{\varepsilon}=\Delta^{out}\times[0,\varepsilon_{0}]
. We proceed again in steps.

Step I: Solve for
r_{3}(t_{3})
,
\varepsilon_{3}(t_{3})
and
T_{3}

We have the following result.

Lemma 5.19
.

Let
r_{3}^{\ast}:=r_{3}(0)
. The transition time
T_{3}
is given by

T_{3}=\frac{1}{2\zeta{r_{3}^{\ast}}^{4}}\left(\rho_{out}^{2}-{r_{3}^{\ast}}^{4}\right),

and for
t_{3}\in[0,T_{3}]
we have

r_{3}(t_{3})=r_{3}^{\ast}(1+2\zeta t_{3})^{1/4},\qquad\varepsilon_{3}(t_{3})=\frac{\zeta}{1+2\zeta t_{3}}.

In particular,
\varepsilon_{3}^{out}(r_{3}^{\ast})=\zeta{r_{3}^{\ast}}^{4}/\rho_{out}^{2}
.

Proof.

The expressions for
r_{3}(t_{3})
and
\varepsilon_{3}(t_{3})
follow by direct integration, and
T_{3}
is obtained from the requirement that
r_{3}(T_{3})=\rho_{out}^{1/2}
. The expression for
\varepsilon_{3}^{out}(r_{3}^{\ast})
follows after evaluating
\varepsilon_{3}(T_{3})
.
∎

Step II: Estimate
A_{1,3}(x_{3},t_{3})

We prove the following result.

Lemma 5.20
.

Fix
\theta_{A}>1/2
and assume that
A_{1,3}^{\ast}\in H_{ul}^{\theta_{A}}
. If
K,\zeta>0
are sufficiently small then for each
\tilde{\sigma}_{0}\geq\tilde{\sigma}>1
there is exists a constant
C>0
such that if initial conditions satisfy

\|A_{1,3}^{\ast}\|_{H_{ul}^{\theta_{A}}}\leq C\mathrm{e}^{-\tilde{\sigma}_{0}\rho_{out}^{2}/2\zeta{r_{3}^{\ast}}^{4}},

(123)

then
A_{1,3}(\cdot,t_{3})\in H_{ul}^{\theta_{A}}
for all
t_{3}\in[0,T_{3}]
, with

\|A_{1,3}(\cdot,T_{3})\|_{H_{ul}^{\theta_{A}}}\leq C\mathrm{e}^{\tilde{\sigma}\rho_{out}^{2}/2\zeta{r_{3}^{\ast}}^{4}}\|A_{1,3}^{\ast}\|_{H_{ul}^{\theta_{A}}}\leq C\mathrm{e}^{-(\tilde{\sigma}_{0}-\tilde{\sigma})\rho_{out}^{2}/2\zeta{r_{3}^{\ast}}^{4}}.

Proof.

The proof proceeds via arguments similar to those used to prove Lemma
5.4
. Substituting the expressions for
\varepsilon_{3}(t_{3})
into the equation for
A_{1,3}
yields

\partial_{t_{3}}A_{1,3}=4\partial_{x_{3}}^{2}A_{1,3}+\left(1-\frac{\varepsilon_{3}(t_{3})}{2}\right)A_{1,3}-3A_{1,3}|A_{1,3}|^{2},\qquad A_{1,3}(x_{3},0)=A_{1,3}^{\ast}(x_{3}).

(124)

Defining an integrating factor
\mathrm{e}^{I_{3}(t_{3})}
via

I_{3}(t_{3})=-\int_{0}^{t_{3}}\frac{\varepsilon_{3}(s_{3})}{2}ds_{3}=-\frac{1}{4}\ln(1+2\zeta t_{3}),\qquad\mathrm{e}^{I_{3}(t_{3})}=\frac{1}{\sqrt[4]{1+2\zeta t_{3}}},

(125)

and applying the transformation
A_{1,3}(x_{3},t_{3})=:\mathrm{e}^{I_{3}(t_{3})}\tilde{A}_{1,3}(x_{3},t_{3})
leads to

\partial_{t_{3}}\tilde{A}_{1,3}=4\partial_{x_{3}}^{2}\tilde{A}_{1,3}+\tilde{A}_{1,3}-3\mathrm{e}^{2I_{3}(t_{3})}\tilde{A}_{1,3}|\tilde{A}_{1,3}|^{2},\qquad\tilde{A}_{1,3}(x_{3},0)=A_{1,3}^{\ast}(x_{3}).

Using the variation of constants formula, this equation can be recast in the integral form

\tilde{A}_{1,3}(\cdot,t_{3})=\mathrm{e}^{t_{3}\Lambda_{3}}A_{1,3}^{\ast}-3\int_{0}^{t_{3}}\mathrm{e}^{(t_{3}-s_{3})\Lambda_{3}}\mathrm{e}^{2I_{3}(s_{3})}\tilde{A}_{1,3}(\cdot,s_{3})|\tilde{A}_{1,3}(\cdot,s_{3})|^{2}ds_{3},

(126)

where
(\mathrm{e}^{t_{3}\Lambda_{3}})_{t_{3}\geq 0}
is the semigroup generated by
\Lambda_{3}:=4\partial_{x_{3}}^{2}+1
. In order to bound
\|\tilde{A}_{1,3}(\cdot,t_{3})\|_{H_{ul}^{\theta_{A}}}
we need to bound
(\mathrm{e}^{t_{3}\Lambda_{3}})_{t_{3}\geq 0}
and the cubic nonlinearity
\tilde{A}_{1,3}(\cdot,s_{3})|\tilde{A}_{1,3}(\cdot,s_{3})|^{2}
in
H_{ul}^{\theta_{A}}
. We shall also use the fact that
\mathrm{e}^{2I_{3}(s_{3})}\leq 1
for all
s_{3}\geq 0
.

The semigroup can be bounded using Lemma
A.2
. We find that for each
\sigma>1
there exists a
C>0
depending on
{\theta_{A}}
such that

\|\mathrm{e}^{t_{3}\Lambda_{3}}\|_{H_{ul}^{\theta_{A}}\to H_{ul}^{\theta_{A}}}\leq C\mathrm{e}^{\sigma t_{3}}

for all
t_{3}\geq 0
.

Using the semigroup bound and Lemma
A.2
we obtain

\|\tilde{A}_{1,3}(\cdot,t_{3})\|_{H^{\theta_{A}}_{ul}}\leq C\mathrm{e}^{\sigma t_{3}}\|A_{1,3}^{\ast}\|_{H^{\theta_{A}}_{ul}}+C\tilde{K}^{2}\int_{0}^{t_{3}}\mathrm{e}^{\sigma(t_{3}-s_{3})}\|\tilde{A}_{1,3}(\cdot,s_{3})\|_{H^{\theta_{A}}_{ul}}\,ds_{3},

for
t_{3}\in[0,\tilde{T}_{3}]
, and applying the Grönwall inequality in Lemma
A.4
leads to

\|\tilde{A}_{1,3}(\cdot,t_{3})\|_{H^{\theta_{A}}_{ul}}\leq C\mathrm{e}^{\tilde{\sigma}t_{3}}\|A_{1,3}^{\ast}\|_{H^{\theta_{A}}_{ul}},

for
t_{3}\in[0,\tilde{T}_{3}]
, where
\tilde{\sigma}:=\sigma+2C\tilde{K}^{2}>1
. Therefore

\|A_{1,3}(\cdot,t_{3})\|_{H^{\theta_{A}}_{ul}}\leq C\frac{\mathrm{e}^{\tilde{\sigma}t_{3}}}{\sqrt[4]{1+2\zeta t_{3}}}\|A_{1,3}^{\ast}\|_{H^{\theta_{A}}_{ul}},

(127)

for
t_{3}\in[0,\tilde{T}_{3}]
. Since the right-hand side is increasing on
t_{3}\in[0,T_{3}]
, it follows that
A_{1,3}(\cdot,t_{3})\in H_{ul}^{\theta_{A}}
for all
t_{3}\in[0,T_{3}]
if the initial condition
A_{1,3}^{\ast}
is sufficiently small in
H_{ul}^{\theta_{A}}
. A sufficient condition is that

\|A_{1,3}(\cdot,T_{3})\|_{H^{\theta_{A}}_{ul}}\leq C\frac{\mathrm{e}^{\tilde{\sigma}T_{3}}}{\sqrt[4]{1+2\zeta T_{3}}}\|A_{1,3}^{\ast}\|_{H^{\theta_{A}}_{ul}}<K.

(128)

This condition is satisfied due to (
123
), which can be seen by substituting the expression for
T_{3}
in Lemma
5.19
. In this case we can set
\tilde{T}_{3}=T_{3}
in (
127
), thereby proving the desired result.
∎

Remark 5.21
.

Since the source term
\varepsilon\mu(x)
does not influence the lowest order approximation, Lemma
5.20
applies whether or not we have
\mu(x)\equiv 0
.

Step III: Estimate
A_{2,3}(x_{3},t_{3})

We prove the following result.

Lemma 5.22
.

Let
\theta_{A}=\theta+3
where
\theta>1/2
and assume that

\|A_{k,3}^{\ast}\|_{H_{ul}^{\theta_{k}}}\leq C\mathrm{e}^{-\tilde{\sigma}_{0}\rho_{out}^{2}/2\zeta{r_{3}^{\ast}}^{4}},

(129)

for
k=1,2
, where
\tilde{\sigma}_{0}>\tilde{\sigma}>\sigma>1
is the same constant as in Lemma
5.20
and
\theta_{k}
is defined in (
74
). Then
A_{2,3}(\cdot,t_{3})\in H_{ul}^{\theta}
for all
t_{3}\in[0,T_{3}]
and

\|A_{2,3}(\cdot,T_{3})\|_{H_{ul}^{\theta}}\leq C{r_{3}^{\ast}}^{-1}\mathrm{e}^{\tilde{\sigma}\rho_{out}^{2}/2\zeta{r_{3}^{\ast}}^{4}}\max_{k=1,2}\|A_{k,3}^{\ast}\|_{H_{ul}^{\theta_{k}}}\leq C\mathrm{e}^{-(\tilde{\sigma}_{0}-\tilde{\sigma})\rho_{out}^{2}/2\zeta{r_{3}^{\ast}}^{4}}.

(130)

Proof.

The proof proceeds via arguments similar to those in the proof of Lemma
5.6
. We have

\partial_{t_{3}}A_{2,3}=4\partial_{x_{3}}^{2}A_{2,3}+\left(1-\frac{\varepsilon_{3}(t_{3})}{2}\right)A_{2,3}-3A_{1,3}(x_{3},t_{3})^{2}\overline{A_{2,3}}-6|A_{1,3}(x_{3},t_{3})|^{2}A_{2,3}-4i\partial_{x_{3}}^{3}A_{1,3}(x_{3},t_{3}),

with
A_{2,3}(x_{3},0)=A_{2,3}^{\ast}(x_{3})
for
t_{3}\in[0,T_{3}]
and
x_{3}\in\mathbb{R}
. Following the transformation
A_{2,3}(x_{3},t_{3})=:\mathrm{e}^{I_{3}(t_{3})}\tilde{A}_{2,3}(x_{3},t_{3})
, where
\mathrm{e}^{I_{3}(t_{3})}
is the same integrating factor used in Step II (recall (
125
)), we obtain

\partial_{t_{3}}\tilde{A}_{2,3}=4\partial_{x_{3}}^{2}\tilde{A}_{2,3}+\tilde{A}_{2,3}-3A_{1,3}(x_{3},t_{3})^{2}\overline{\tilde{A}_{2,3}}-6|A_{1,3}(x_{3},t_{3})|^{2}\tilde{A}_{2,3}-4i\mathrm{e}^{-I_{3}(t_{3})}\partial_{x_{3}}^{3}A_{1,3}(x_{3},t_{3}),

with
A_{2,3}(x_{3},0)=A_{2,3}^{\ast}(x_{3})
. After applying the variation of constants formula we obtain

\begin{split}\tilde{A}_{2,3}&(\cdot,t_{3})=\mathrm{e}^{t_{3}\Lambda_{3}}A_{2,3}^{\ast}\ -\\
\int_{0}^{t_{3}}&\mathrm{e}^{(t_{3}-s_{3})\Lambda_{3}}\left(3A_{1,3}(\cdot,s_{3})^{2}\overline{\tilde{A}_{2,3}}(\cdot,s_{3})+6|A_{1,3}(\cdot,s_{3})|^{2}\tilde{A}_{2,3}(\cdot,s_{3})+4i\mathrm{e}^{-I_{3}(s_{3})}\partial_{x_{3}}^{3}A_{1,3}(\cdot,s_{3})\right)ds_{3},\end{split}

(131)

where
\Lambda_{3}=4\partial_{x_{3}}^{2}+1
as in Step II. We have the same bound for the semigroup as in Step II, i.e. for each
\sigma>1
there is a constant
C>0
depending on
\theta
such that
\|\mathrm{e}^{t_{3}\Lambda_{3}}\|_{H_{ul}^{\theta}\to H_{ul}^{\theta}}\leq C\mathrm{e}^{\sigma t_{3}}
. Given the bounds for
A_{1,3}
in Lemma
5.20
and its proof (in particular (
127
)), it follows via arguments analogous to those applied in the proof of Lemma
5.6
that for all
t_{3}\in[0,\tilde{T}_{2,3}]
we have

\begin{split}\|\tilde{A}_{2,3}(\cdot,t_{3})\|_{H_{ul}^{\theta}}&\leq C\mathrm{e}^{\sigma t_{3}}\|A_{2,3}^{\ast}\|_{H_{ul}^{\theta}}+C\mathrm{e}^{\tilde{\sigma}t_{3}}\|A_{1,3}^{\ast}\|_{H_{ul}^{\theta_{A}}}\\
&+Ci\mathrm{e}^{\sigma t_{3}}\|A_{1,3}^{\ast}\|_{H_{ul}^{\theta}}^{2}\Gamma\left(\frac{1}{2},-(-\sigma+2\tilde{\sigma})\left(\frac{1}{2\zeta}+s_{3}\right)\right)\bigg|_{t_{3}}^{0},\end{split}

(132)

where
-\sigma+2\tilde{\sigma}=\sigma+4C\tilde{K}^{2}>1
and
-\sigma+\tilde{\sigma}=2C\tilde{K}^{2}\in(0,1)
. A direct calculation using the upper gamma function asymptotics in Appendix
A
(equation (
166
)) shows that

\|\tilde{A}_{2,3}(\cdot,t_{3})\|_{H_{ul}^{\theta}}\leq C\mathrm{e}^{\tilde{\sigma}t_{3}}\max_{k=1,2}\|A_{k,3}^{\ast}\|_{H_{ul}^{\theta_{k}}}+C\mathrm{e}^{2\tilde{\sigma}t_{3}}\|A_{1,3}^{\ast}\|_{H_{ul}^{\theta}}^{2}\leq C\mathrm{e}^{\tilde{\sigma}t_{3}}\max_{k=1,2}\|A_{k,3}^{\ast}\|_{H_{ul}^{\theta_{k}}},

(133)

for
t_{3}\in[0,\tilde{T}_{2,3}]
, where we used the bound on
\|A_{1,3}^{\ast}\|_{H_{ul}^{\theta}}
from (
129
) in the second inequality. Using the bounds in (
129
) again leads to

\|\tilde{A}_{2,3}(\cdot,T_{3})\|_{H_{ul}^{\theta}}\leq C\mathrm{e}^{-(\tilde{\sigma}_{0}-\tilde{\sigma})\rho_{out}^{2}/2\zeta{r_{3}^{\ast}}^{4}}.

(134)

Since (
132
) is increasing on
t_{3}\in[0,T_{3}]
and (
134
) is bounded because
\tilde{\sigma}_{0}>\tilde{\sigma}
, we can ensure that
\tilde{T}_{2,3}=T_{3}
by choosing
\vartheta>0
sufficiently small. The bound in (
130
) follows after multiplying through by the integrating factor.
∎

Step IV: Estimate
A_{j,3}(x_{3},t_{3})
,
j=3,\ldots,N-2

Similarly to the Step IV analysis in charts
\mathcal{K}_{1}
and
\mathcal{K}_{2}
we write
a_{j,3}=:a_{j,3}^{L}+a_{j,3}^{N}
, where

a_{j,3}^{L}(\tilde{A}_{j,3},\overline{\tilde{A}_{j,3}})=\left(\alpha_{1}A_{1,3}^{2}+\alpha_{2}|A_{1,3}|^{2}+\alpha_{3}\overline{A_{1,3}}^{2}\right)\tilde{A}_{j,3}+\left(\beta_{1}A_{1,3}^{2}+\beta_{2}|A_{1,3}|^{2}+\beta_{3}\overline{A_{1,3}}^{2}\right)\overline{\tilde{A}_{j,3}},

and
a_{j,3}^{N}
depends only modulation functions
A_{m^{\prime}j^{\prime},3}
at lower orders with
\alpha(m^{\prime})+j^{\prime}<j
.

We prove the following result.

Lemma 5.23
.

Let
\theta_{A}=\theta_{A}(j)=3(j-1)+\theta
where
j\in\{3,\ldots,N-2\}
and
\theta>1/2
, assume

\|a_{j,3}^{N}(\cdot,t_{3})\|_{H_{ul}^{\theta}}\leq C\max_{k=1,\ldots,j-1}\|A_{k,3}(\cdot,t_{3})\|_{H_{ul}^{\theta_{j,k}}}^{3},

(135)

and that

\|A_{k,3}^{\ast}\|_{H_{ul}^{\theta_{k,j}}}\leq C\mathrm{e}^{-\tilde{\sigma}_{0}\rho_{out}^{2}/2\zeta{r_{3}^{\ast}}^{4}},

(136)

for all
k=1,\ldots,j
, where
\tilde{\sigma}_{0}>\tilde{\sigma}>\sigma>1
is the same constant as in Lemmas
5.20
-
5.22
and
\theta_{k,j}
is given by (
84
). Then
A_{j,3}(\cdot,t_{3})\in H_{ul}^{\theta}
for all
t_{3}\in[0,T_{3}]
and

\|A_{j,3}(\cdot,T_{3})\|_{H_{ul}^{\theta}}\leq C\mathrm{e}^{-(\tilde{\sigma}_{0}-\tilde{\sigma})\rho_{out}^{2}/2\zeta{r_{3}^{\ast}}^{4}}.

(137)

Proof.

We proceed similarly to the proof of Lemmas
5.7
,
5.16
, and show that
A_{j,3}(\cdot,t_{3})\in H_{ul}^{\theta}
for all
j=1,\ldots,N-2
using an inductive argument over
k=3,\ldots,j-1\leq N-3
.
As an induction hypothesis we assume the existence of solutions
A_{k,3}(\cdot,t_{3})\in H_{ul}^{\theta_{k,j}}
for each
k=3,\ldots,j-1\leq N-3
, for all
t_{3}\in[0,T_{3}]
. As in the proofs of Lemmas
5.7
and
5.16
, we skip the base case for brevity since it is similar to the inductive step.

The equation for
A_{j,3}
is given by

\partial_{t_{3}}A_{j,3}=4\partial_{x_{3}}^{2}A_{j,3}+\left(1-\frac{\varepsilon_{3}(t_{3})}{2}\right)A_{j,1}-a_{j,3}-4i\partial_{x_{3}}^{3}A_{j-1,3}-\partial_{x_{3}}^{4}A_{j-2,3},\qquad A_{j,3}(x_{3},0)=A_{j,3}^{\ast}(x_{3}),

for all
x_{3}\in\mathbb{R}
and
t_{3}\in[0,T_{3}]
. Many of the estimates below are obtained using arguments similar to those used in Steps II-III, so we present fewer details. Note that
\partial_{x_{3}}^{3}A_{j-1,3}
and
\partial_{x_{3}}^{4}A_{j-2,3}
can be considered as source terms, since we are given solutions
A_{j-1,3}(\cdot,t_{3})\in H_{ul}^{\theta_{j-1,j}}
and
A_{j-2,3}(\cdot,t_{3})\in H_{ul}^{\theta_{j-2,j}}
for all
t_{3}\in[0,T_{3}]
via Lemma
5.20
, Lemma
5.22
or induction hypothesis depending on
j
.

Setting
A_{j,3}(x_{3},t_{3})=:\mathrm{e}^{I_{3}(t_{3})}\tilde{A}_{j,3}(x_{3},t_{3})
where
I_{3}(t_{3})
is given by (
125
) leads to

\partial_{t_{3}}\tilde{A}_{j,3}=4\partial_{x_{3}}^{2}\tilde{A}_{j,3}-\tilde{A}_{j,3}-a_{j,3}^{L}(\tilde{A}_{j,3},\overline{\tilde{A}_{j,3}})-\mathrm{e}^{-I_{3}(t_{3})}\left(a^{N}_{j,3}+4i\partial_{x_{3}}^{3}A_{j-1,3}+\partial_{x_{3}}^{4}A_{j-2,3}\right),

together with
\tilde{A}_{j,3}(x_{3},0)=A_{j,3}^{\ast}(x_{3})
. We apply the variation of constants formula and split the solution into two parts
\tilde{A}_{j,3}=:\tilde{A}_{j,3,h}+\tilde{A}_{j,3,s}
, where

\begin{split}\tilde{A}_{j,3,h}(\cdot,t_{3})&=\mathrm{e}^{t_{3}\Lambda_{3}}A_{j,3}^{\ast}-\int_{0}^{t_{3}}\mathrm{e}^{(t_{3}-s_{3})\Lambda_{3}}a_{j,3}^{L}(\tilde{A}_{j,3}(\cdot,s_{3}),\overline{\tilde{A}_{j,3}}(\cdot,s_{3}))ds_{3},\\
\tilde{A}_{j,3,s}(\cdot,t_{3})&=-\int_{0}^{t_{3}}\mathrm{e}^{(t_{3}-s_{3})\Lambda_{3}}\mathrm{e}^{-I_{3}(s_{3})}\left(a^{N}_{j,3}(\cdot,s_{3})+4i\partial_{x_{3}}^{3}A_{j-1,3}(\cdot,s_{3})+\partial_{x_{3}}^{4}A_{j-2,3}(\cdot,s_{3})\right)ds_{3},\end{split}

(138)

for
t_{3}\in[0,T_{3}]
. Arguments analogous to those which led to the bound for
\|A_{2,3}(\cdot,t_{3})\|_{H_{ul}^{\theta}}
in Lemma
5.22
show that
A_{j,3,h}(\cdot,t_{3})=\mathrm{e}^{I_{3}(t_{3})}\tilde{A}_{j,3,h}(\cdot,t_{3})
has a similar bound. Specifically, we have

\begin{split}\|\tilde{A}_{j,3,h}(\cdot,t_{3})\|_{H_{ul}^{\theta}}&\leq C\mathrm{e}^{\sigma t_{3}}\|A_{j,3}^{\ast}\|_{H_{ul}^{\theta}}+Ci\mathrm{e}^{\sigma t_{3}}\|A_{1,3}^{\ast}\|_{H_{ul}^{\theta}}^{2}\Gamma\left(\frac{1}{2},-(-\sigma+2\tilde{\sigma})\left(\frac{1}{2\zeta}+s_{3}\right)\right)\bigg|_{t_{3}}^{0},\\
&\leq C\mathrm{e}^{\sigma t_{3}}\|A_{j,3}^{\ast}\|_{H_{ul}^{\theta}}+C\mathrm{e}^{2\tilde{\sigma}t_{3}}\|A_{1,3}^{\ast}\|_{H_{ul}^{\theta}}^{2}\\
&\leq C\mathrm{e}^{\sigma t_{3}}\|A_{j,3}^{\ast}\|_{H_{ul}^{\theta}}+C\mathrm{e}^{\tilde{\sigma}t_{3}}\|A_{1,3}^{\ast}\|_{H_{ul}^{\theta}}\\
&\leq C\mathrm{e}^{\tilde{\sigma}t_{3}}\max_{k=1,\ldots,j}\|A_{k,3}^{\ast}\|_{H_{ul}^{\theta}},\end{split}

(139)

for all
t_{3}\in[0,\tilde{T}_{j,3}]
, where we used the gamma function asymptotics (
166
) in Appendix
A
in the second inequality, and the bound for
A_{1,3}^{\ast}
in (
136
) in the second to last inequality. Using the expression for
T_{3}
in Lemma
5.19
and the bounds (
136
), we obtain

\|A_{j,3,h}(\cdot,T_{3})\|_{H_{ul}^{\theta}}\leq C\mathrm{e}^{-(\tilde{\sigma}_{0}-\tilde{\sigma})\rho_{out}^{2}/2\zeta{r_{3}^{\ast}}^{4}}.

(140)

Now consider the source term
\tilde{A}_{j,3,s}
. Using the same bound for the semigroup as in Steps II-III we obtain the estimate

\begin{split}\|\tilde{A}_{j,3,s}(\cdot,t_{3})\|_{H_{ul}^{\theta}}&\leq\\
C\mathrm{e}^{\sigma t_{3}}&\int_{0}^{t_{3}}\mathrm{e}^{-\sigma s_{3}}\sqrt[4]{1+2\zeta s_{3}}\left\|a^{N}_{j,3}(\cdot,s_{3})+4i\partial_{x_{3}}^{3}A_{j-1,3}(\cdot,s_{3})+\partial_{x_{3}}^{4}A_{j-2,3}(\cdot,s_{3})\right\|_{H_{ul}^{\theta}}ds_{3},\end{split}

(141)

for all
t_{3}\in[0,\tilde{T}_{j,3}]
. From this point on the proof resembles the part of the proof of Lemma
5.7
devoted to the proving the bound (
83
) when
\mu(x)\equiv 0
. We show that

\|\tilde{A}_{j,3,s}(\cdot,t_{3})\|_{H_{ul}^{\theta}}\leq C\mathrm{e}^{-(\tilde{\sigma}_{0}-\tilde{\sigma})\rho_{out}^{2}/2\zeta{r_{3}^{\ast}}^{4}},

(142)

by an inductive argument over
k=3,\ldots,j
. The idea is to bound each
\|\tilde{A}_{k,3,s}(\cdot,t_{3})\|_{H_{ul}^{\theta}}
using bounds established for lower orders.

In order to prove the base case for
k=3
, we use the bounds

\|A_{1,3}(\cdot,t_{3})\|_{H_{ul}^{\theta_{A}(j)}}\leq C\frac{\mathrm{e}^{\tilde{\sigma}t_{3}}}{\sqrt[4]{1+2\zeta t_{3}}}\|A_{1,3}^{\ast}\|_{H_{ul}^{\theta_{A}(j)}},\qquad\|A_{2,3}(\cdot,t_{3})\|_{H_{ul}^{\theta_{2,j}}}\leq\\
C\frac{\mathrm{e}^{\tilde{\sigma}t_{3}}}{\sqrt[4]{1+2\zeta t_{3}}}\max_{i=1,2}\|A_{k,3}^{\ast}\|_{H_{ul}^{\theta_{k,j}}},

which apply for all
t_{3}\in[0,T_{3}]
due to the proofs of Lemmas
5.20
and
5.22
(c.f. equations (
127
) and (
133
) respectively).

In order to obtain a bound on the integrand in (
141
) we use (
135
), which implies

\begin{split}\|a^{N}_{3,3}(\cdot,s_{3})\|_{H_{ul}^{\theta_{3,j}}}&\leq C\max_{i=1,2}\|A_{i,3}(\cdot,t_{3})\|_{H_{ul}^{\theta_{i,j}}}^{3}\\
&\leq C\frac{\mathrm{e}^{3\tilde{\sigma}t_{3}}}{(1+2\zeta t_{3})^{3/4}}\max_{i=1,2}\|A_{i,3}^{\ast}\|_{H_{ul}^{\theta_{i,j}}}^{3}\\
&\leq C\frac{\mathrm{e}^{\tilde{\sigma}t_{3}}}{\sqrt[4]{1+2\zeta t_{3}}}\max_{i=1,2}\|A_{i,3}^{\ast}\|_{H_{ul}^{\theta_{i,j}}},\end{split}

where we used Lemma
5.20
in the second inequality and (
136
) together with
(1+2\zeta t_{3})^{-3/4}\leq(1+2\zeta t_{3})^{-1/4}
in the last inequality. The derivative terms can be bounded using the bounds for
A_{1,3}
and
A_{2,3}
above. Specifically,

\begin{split}\|\partial_{x_{3}}^{3}A_{2,3}(\cdot,s_{3})\|_{H_{ul}^{\theta_{3,j}}}&\leq C\|A_{2,3}(\cdot,s_{3})\|_{H_{ul}^{\theta_{2,j}}}\leq C\frac{\mathrm{e}^{\tilde{\sigma}t_{3}}}{\sqrt[4]{1+2\zeta t_{3}}}\max_{i=1,2}\|A_{i,3}^{\ast}\|_{H_{ul}^{\theta_{i,j}}},\\
\|\partial_{x_{3}}^{4}A_{1,3}(\cdot,s_{3})\|_{H_{ul}^{\theta_{3,j}}}&\leq C\|A_{1,3}(\cdot,s_{3})\|_{H_{ul}^{\theta_{A}(j)}}\leq C\frac{\mathrm{e}^{\tilde{\sigma}t_{3}}}{\sqrt[4]{1+2\zeta t_{3}}}\|A_{1,3}^{\ast}\|_{H_{ul}^{\theta_{A}(j)}},\end{split}

for
s_{3}\in[0,T_{3}]
. It follows that

\left\|a^{N}_{3,3}(\cdot,s_{3})+4i\partial_{x_{3}}^{3}A_{2,3}(\cdot,s_{3})+\partial_{x_{3}}^{4}A_{1,3}(\cdot,s_{3})\right\|_{H_{ul}^{\theta_{3,j}}}\leq C\frac{\mathrm{e}^{\tilde{\sigma}t_{3}}}{\sqrt[4]{1+2\zeta t_{3}}}\max_{i=1,2}\|A_{i,3}^{\ast}\|_{H_{ul}^{\theta_{i,j}}},

for all
s_{3}\in[0,\tilde{T}_{3,3}]
. Using this to bound (
141
) with
j=3
, we obtain

\|\tilde{A}_{3,3,s}(\cdot,t_{3})\|_{H_{ul}^{\theta_{3,j}}}\leq C\mathrm{e}^{\sigma t_{3}}\max_{i=1,2}\|A_{i,3}^{\ast}\|_{H_{ul}^{\theta_{i,j}}}\int_{0}^{t_{3}}\mathrm{e}^{(\tilde{\sigma}-\sigma)s_{3}}ds_{3}\leq C\mathrm{e}^{\tilde{\sigma}t_{3}}\max_{i=1,2}\|A_{i,3}^{\ast}\|_{H_{ul}^{\theta_{i,j}}},

for
t_{3}\in[0,\tilde{T}_{3,3}]
.

Multiplying through by the integrating factor and using the bounds on the initial conditions in (
136
) we obtain

\|A_{3,3,s}(\cdot,T_{3})\|_{H_{ul}^{\theta_{3,j}}}\leq C\mathrm{e}^{-(\tilde{\sigma}_{0}-\tilde{\sigma})\rho_{out}^{2}/2\zeta{r_{3}^{\ast}}^{4}},

(143)

which is valid for each
\tilde{\sigma}_{0}>\tilde{\sigma}
if
\vartheta>0
is sufficiently small. The desired bound for
\|A_{3,3}(\cdot,t_{3})\|_{H_{ul}^{\theta_{3,j}}}
is obtained using (
140
), (
143
) and the triangle inequality. Note that
\tilde{T}_{3,3}=T_{3}
as required since the right-hand sides in (
140
) and (
143
) are bounded below
\tilde{K}
.

We turn now to the induction step, and assume that (
142
) holds for
k=3,\ldots,j-1
. Similarly to the proof of Lemma
5.7
, we present fewer details since the structure of the argument is the same as the base case. Using (
135
), the induction hypothesis and the bounds in (
136
) we obtain

\|a^{N}_{j,3}(\cdot,s_{3})\|_{H_{ul}^{\theta}}\leq C\frac{\mathrm{e}^{3\tilde{\sigma}s_{1}}}{(1+2\zeta s_{3})^{3/4}}\max_{i=1,\ldots,j-1}\|A_{i,3}^{\ast}\|_{H_{ul}^{\theta_{i,j}}}^{3}\leq C\frac{\mathrm{e}^{\tilde{\sigma}s_{3}}}{\sqrt[4]{1+2\zeta s_{3}}}\max_{i=1,\ldots,j-1}\|A_{i,3}^{\ast}\|_{H_{ul}^{\theta_{i,j}}},

for
s_{3}\in[0,T_{3}]
. The derivative terms are controlled because we assume sufficient regularity, so that the induction hypothesis implies

\begin{split}\|\partial_{x_{3}}^{3}A_{j-1,3}(\cdot,s_{3})\|_{H_{ul}^{\theta}}&\leq C\|A_{j-1,3}(\cdot,s_{3})\|_{H_{ul}^{\theta_{j-1,j}}}\leq C\frac{\mathrm{e}^{\tilde{\sigma}s_{3}}}{\sqrt[4]{1+2\zeta s_{3}}}\max_{i=1,\ldots,j-1}\|A_{i,3}^{\ast}\|_{H_{ul}^{\theta_{i,j}}},\\
\|\partial_{x_{3}}^{4}A_{j-2,3}(\cdot,s_{3})\|_{H_{ul}^{\theta}}&\leq C\|A_{j-2,3}(\cdot,s_{3})\|_{H_{ul}^{\theta_{j-2,j}}}\leq C\frac{\mathrm{e}^{\tilde{\sigma}s_{3}}}{\sqrt[4]{1+2\zeta s_{3}}}\max_{i=1,\ldots,j-2}\|A_{i,3}^{\ast}\|_{H_{ul}^{\theta_{i,j}}},\end{split}

for
s_{3}\in[0,T_{3}]
. From here the bound for
\|A_{j,3}(\cdot,t_{3})\|_{H_{ul}^{\theta}}
on
t_{3}\in[0,T_{3}]
follows analogously to the base case
k=3
. ∎

5.4.1
Step V: Estimate
\psi_{3}

We need the following result before we can describe the dynamics of
\psi_{3}
. As in the chart
\mathcal{K}_{2}
and
\mathcal{K}_{3}
analyses, we reinstate the full notation
A_{\pm 1j,3}
instead of
A_{j,3}
in order to distinguish modulation functions with
|m|=1
and
|m|\neq 1
,
and recall that
\textbf{A}_{mj,3}
denotes the set of lower order critical modulation functions
A_{\pm 1j^{\prime},3}
with
j^{\prime}\in\{1,\ldots,\alpha(m)+j-1\}
.

Lemma 5.24
.

Let
\theta_{A}=3(N-3)+\theta
where
\theta>1/2
and assume that initial conditions
A_{\pm 1j,3}^{\ast}
satisfy (
136
). Then the conditions on
a_{j,3}^{N}
for the applicability of Lemma
5.23
are satisfied, and
A_{\pm 1j,3}(\cdot,t_{3})\in H_{ul}^{\theta_{j,N-2}}
for all
j=1,\ldots,N-2
and
t_{3}\in[0,T_{3}]
. Moreover,

A_{mj,3}(\cdot,t_{3})=g_{mj,3}^{gl}\left(\textbf{{A}}_{mj,3}(\cdot,t_{3}),\varepsilon_{3}(t_{3})\right)\in H_{ul}^{\theta_{\alpha(m)+j,N}},

(144)

and

\big\|g_{mj,3}^{gl}\left(\textbf{{A}}_{mj,3}(\cdot,T_{3}),\varepsilon_{3}(T_{3})\right)\big\|_{H_{ul}^{\theta_{\alpha(m)+j,N}}}\leq C\mathrm{e}^{-(\tilde{\sigma}_{0}-\tilde{\sigma})\rho_{out}^{2}/2\zeta{r_{3}^{\ast}}^{4}},

(145)

for all
t_{2}\in[0,T_{2}]
,
m\in I_{N}\setminus\{-1,1\}
and
j=1,\ldots,\tilde{\alpha}(m)
.

Proof.

The proof proceeds similarly to the (second part of the) proof of Lemma
5.9
, so we omit the details for brevity. It is necessary to assume the initial condition bounds (
136
) in order to apply Lemmas
5.20
,
5.22
and
5.23
.
∎

Combining the results of Lemmas
5.19
,
5.20
,
5.22
,
5.23
and
5.24
we obtain the following result, which summarises the dynamics in chart
\mathcal{K}_{3}
.

Proposition 5.25
.

Let
\theta_{A}=1+3(N-3)+\theta
where
\theta>1/2
and assume that initial conditions are small according to (
136
). Then for
\zeta,K>0
sufficiently small but fixed we have
\psi_{3}(\cdot,t_{3})\in H_{ul}^{\theta}
for all
t_{3}\in[0,T_{3}]
. In particular, the map
\pi_{3}:\Sigma_{3}^{in}\to\Sigma_{3}^{out}
is well-defined and given by

\pi_{3}:\left(\psi_{3}^{\ast},r_{3}^{\ast},\zeta\right)\mapsto\left(\psi_{3}(\cdot,T_{3}),\rho_{out}^{1/2},\zeta\frac{{r_{3}^{\ast}}^{4}}{\rho_{out}^{2}}\right),

(146)

where
\psi_{3}(x,T_{3})
satisfies

\|\psi_{3}(\cdot,T_{3})\|_{H_{ul}^{\theta}}\leq C\mathrm{e}^{-(\tilde{\sigma}_{0}-\tilde{\sigma})\rho_{out}^{2}/2\zeta{r_{3}^{\ast}}^{4}},

(147)

with
\tilde{\sigma}_{0}>\tilde{\sigma}>\sigma>1
.

Proof.

The conditions of Proposition
5.25
imply that Lemmas
5.19
,
5.20
,
5.22
,
5.23
and
5.24
apply. It follows that all modulation functions
A_{mj,3}(\cdot,t_{3})\in H_{ul}^{\theta+1}
for all
t_{3}\in[0,T_{3}]
, implying that
\psi_{3}(\cdot,t_{3})\in H_{ul}^{\theta}
for all
t_{3}\in[0,T_{3}]
(recall the definition of
\psi_{3}
in (
45
)).

The form of the map in (
146
) follows from Lemma
5.19
. To verify the bound in (
147
), notice that Lemmas
5.20
,
5.22
,
5.23
and
5.24
imply that for each
m\in I_{N}
we have

\|A_{mj,3}(\cdot,T_{3})\|_{H_{ul}^{\theta+1}}\leq C\mathrm{e}^{-(\tilde{\sigma}_{0}-\tilde{\sigma})\rho_{out}^{2}/2\zeta{r_{3}^{\ast}}^{4}},

for all
j=1,\ldots,\tilde{\alpha}(m)
. Thus

\begin{split}\|\psi_{3}(\cdot,T_{3})\|_{H_{ul}^{\theta}}&\leq\frac{1}{r_{3}(T_{3})}\sum_{m\in I_{N}}\sum_{j=1}^{\tilde{\alpha}(m)}r_{3}(T_{3})^{\alpha(m)+j}\|A_{mj,3}(r_{3}(T_{s})\cdot,T_{3})\mathrm{e}^{imx}\|_{H_{ul}^{\theta}}\\
&\leq C\max_{m\in I_{N},j\in\{1,\ldots,\tilde{\alpha}(m)\}}\|A_{mj,3}(\cdot,T_{3})\|_{H_{ul}^{\theta+1}}\\
&\leq C\mathrm{e}^{-(\tilde{\sigma}_{0}-\tilde{\sigma})\rho_{out}^{2}/2\zeta{r_{3}^{\ast}}^{4}},\end{split}

as required. The bound (
147
) implies that
\pi_{3}
is well-defined because
\|\psi_{3}(\cdot,T_{3})\|_{H_{ul}^{\theta}}\leq K
for all
r_{3}^{\ast}\in[0,\zeta^{-1/4}\vartheta]
with
\vartheta>0
sufficiently small.
∎

5.5
Proof of Lemma
3.16

We are now in a position to prove Lemma
3.16
by combining results obtained for the case
\mu(x)\equiv 0
in each chart
\mathcal{K}_{l}
,
l=1,2,3
. The fact that
\Psi(\cdot,t)=r(\bar{t})\psi(\cdot,\bar{t})\in H_{ul}^{\theta}
for all
t\in[0,T]
follows directly from the fact that
\psi_{l}(\cdot,t_{l})\in H_{ul}^{\theta}
for all
t_{l}\in[0,T_{l}]
in each chart
\mathcal{K}_{l}
; recall Propositions
5.11
,
5.18
and
5.25
. It therefore remains to prove the bound (
41
).

Similarly to the proof of Lemma
3.14
in Section
5.3
, we consider an initial condition
(\Psi^{\ast},-\rho_{in})\in\Delta^{in}
, which can be rewritten as initial condition
(\psi_{1}^{\ast},\sqrt{\rho_{in}},\varepsilon_{1}^{\ast})\in\Sigma_{1}^{in}
in chart
\mathcal{K}_{1}
using (
120
). Since
\mu(x)\equiv 0
, Proposition
5.11
implies that solutions are exponentially small in
H_{ul}^{\theta}
at
\Sigma_{2}^{in}=\kappa_{12}(\Sigma_{1}^{out})
. This time, we obtain the following bounds for the initial modulation functions
A_{mj,2}^{\ast}=\zeta^{-(\alpha(m)+j)/4}A_{mj,1}^{\ast}(\cdot,T_{1})
in chart
\mathcal{K}_{2}
using the assertions of Lemmas
5.4
,
5.6
,
5.7
and
5.9
relevant to the case
\mu(x)\equiv 0
:

\begin{split}&\|A_{1,2}^{\ast}\|_{H_{ul}^{\theta_{A}}}=\zeta^{-1/4}\|A_{1,1}(\cdot,T_{1})\|_{H_{ul}^{\theta_{A}}}\leq C\mathrm{e}^{-\kappa/2\varepsilon_{1}^{\ast}}\|A_{1,1}^{\ast}\|_{H_{ul}^{\theta_{A}}},\\
&\|A_{2,2}^{\ast}\|_{H_{ul}^{\theta_{2,N-2}}}=\zeta^{-1/2}\|A_{2,1}(\cdot,T_{1})\|_{H_{ul}^{\theta_{2,N-2}}}\leq C\mathrm{e}^{-\kappa/2\varepsilon_{1}^{\ast}}\max_{k=1,2}\|A_{k,1}^{\ast}\|_{H_{ul}^{\theta_{k,N-2}}},\\
&\|A_{j,2}^{\ast}\|_{H_{ul}^{\theta_{j,N-2}}}=\zeta^{-j/4}\|A_{j,1}(\cdot,T_{1})\|_{H_{ul}^{\theta_{j,N-2}}}\leq C\mathrm{e}^{-\kappa/2\varepsilon_{1}^{\ast}}\max_{k=1,\ldots,j}\|A_{k,1}^{\ast}\|_{H_{ul}^{\theta_{k,N-2}}},\\
&\|A_{mj,2}^{\ast}\|_{H_{ul}^{\theta_{\alpha(m)+j,N}}}=\zeta^{-(\alpha(m)+j)/4}\|A_{mj,1}(\cdot,T_{1})\|_{H_{ul}^{\theta_{\alpha(m)+j,N}}}\leq C\mathrm{e}^{-\kappa/2\varepsilon_{1}^{\ast}}\max_{k=1,\ldots,j-2}\|A_{k,1}^{\ast}\|_{H_{ul}^{\theta_{k,N-2}}},\end{split}

(148)

where
j\geq 3
in the second-to-last line and
|m|\neq 1
in the last.
The evolution from
\Sigma_{2}^{in}
up to
\Sigma_{2}^{out}
is understood in chart
\mathcal{K}_{2}
and summarised in Proposition
5.18
. Bounds for the initial modulation functions
A_{mj,3}^{\ast}=\zeta^{(\alpha(m)+j)/4}A_{mj,2}^{\ast}(\cdot,T_{2})
in chart
\mathcal{K}_{3}
are obtained via Lemmas
5.14
,
5.15
,
5.16
and
5.17
. These bounds can be formulated in terms of initial data in
\mathcal{K}_{1}
using (
148
). We obtain

\begin{split}&\|A_{1,3}^{\ast}\|_{H_{ul}^{\theta_{A}}}=\zeta^{1/4}\|A_{1,2}(\cdot,T_{2})\|_{H_{ul}^{\theta_{A}}}\leq C\mathrm{e}^{-\kappa/2\varepsilon_{1}^{\ast}}\|A_{1,1}^{\ast}\|_{H_{ul}^{\theta_{A}}},\\
&\|A_{2,3}^{\ast}\|_{H_{ul}^{\theta_{2,N-2}}}=\zeta^{1/2}\|A_{2,2}(\cdot,T_{2})\|_{H_{ul}^{\theta_{2,N-2}}}\leq C\mathrm{e}^{-\kappa/2\varepsilon_{1}^{\ast}}\max_{k=1,2}\|A_{k,1}^{\ast}\|_{H_{ul}^{\theta_{k,N-2}}},\\
&\|A_{j,3}^{\ast}\|_{H_{ul}^{\theta_{j,N-2}}}=\zeta^{j/4}\|A_{j,2}(\cdot,T_{2})\|_{H_{ul}^{\theta_{j,N-2}}}\leq C\mathrm{e}^{-\kappa/2\varepsilon_{1}^{\ast}}\max_{k=1,\ldots,j}\|A_{k,1}^{\ast}\|_{H_{ul}^{\theta_{k,N-2}}},\\
&\|A_{mj,3}^{\ast}\|_{H_{ul}^{\theta_{\alpha(m)+j,N}}}=\zeta^{(\alpha(m)+j)/4}\|A_{mj,2}(\cdot,T_{2})\|_{H_{ul}^{\theta_{\alpha(m)+j,N}}}\leq C\mathrm{e}^{-\kappa/2\varepsilon_{1}^{\ast}}\max_{k=1,\ldots,j-2}\|A_{k,1}^{\ast}\|_{H_{ul}^{\theta_{k,N-2}}},\end{split}

(149)

where again,
j\geq 3
in the second-to-last line and
m\neq 1
in the last. The idea from here is to apply Proposition
5.25
. In order to do so, we need to check the bounds (
136
).

Since the argument is the same for each
A_{j,1}^{\ast}
, we present the details for
j=1
only. A sufficient condition for (
136
) to be satisfied is

\|A_{1,3}^{\ast}\|_{H_{ul}^{\theta}}\leq C\mathrm{e}^{-\kappa/2\varepsilon_{1}^{\ast}}\|A_{1,1}^{\ast}\|_{H_{ul}^{\theta}}\leq C\mathrm{e}^{-\tilde{\sigma}_{0}\rho_{out}^{2}/2\zeta{r_{3}^{\ast}}^{4}},

(150)

which is satisfied for all
\varepsilon_{1}^{\ast}
and
r_{3}^{\ast}
sufficiently small if

\frac{\kappa}{2\varepsilon_{1}^{\ast}}\geq\frac{\tilde{\sigma}_{0}\rho_{out}^{2}}{2\zeta{r_{3}^{\ast}}^{4}}.

(151)

It follows from the form of the maps
\pi_{1}
and
\pi_{2}
in Propositions
5.11
and
5.18
respectively that

r_{3}^{\ast}=\zeta^{-1/4}r_{2}(T_{2})=\zeta^{-1/4}r_{2}^{\ast}=r_{1}(T_{1})=\rho_{in}^{1/2}\sqrt[4]{\frac{\varepsilon_{1}^{\ast}}{\zeta}}.

(152)

We also recall that for sufficiently small (but fixed)
K>0
, the constants
\kappa\in(0,1)
and
\tilde{\sigma}_{0}>1
can be chosen arbitrarily close to
1
. It follows that (
151
) (and therefore (
150
)) is satisfied if

\frac{\rho_{out}}{\rho_{in}}\leq\sqrt{\frac{\kappa}{\tilde{\sigma}_{0}}}=:1-\omega,

where
\omega\in(0,1)
can be chosen arbitrarily close to
1
if
K>0
is fixed but sufficiently small, which is precisely the assumption in the statement of Lemma
3.16
.

The preceding arguments show that Proposition
5.25
applies. Setting
\tilde{\sigma}_{0}=\kappa(\rho_{in}/\rho_{out})^{2}
in order to maximise the contraction, we obtain the following via (
147
), (
152
) and the blow-down transformation
\varepsilon_{1}^{\ast}=\varepsilon/\rho_{in}^{2}
:

\|\psi_{3}(\cdot,T_{3})\|_{H_{ul}^{\theta}}\leq C\exp\left(-\frac{\kappa}{2\varepsilon}\left(\rho_{in}^{2}-\frac{\tilde{\sigma}}{\kappa}\rho_{out}^{2}\right)\right),

where
\tilde{\sigma}/\kappa>1
can be chosen arbitrarily close to
1
if
K>0
is sufficiently small. Setting
\kappa_{-}:=\kappa
and
\kappa_{+}:=\tilde{\sigma}/\kappa
and applying the blow-down transformation

\psi_{3}(\cdot,T_{3})=\frac{\Psi(\cdot,T)}{\sqrt{\rho_{out}}}

yields the desired result.
∎

6
Proof of Theorem
3.20

In this section we prove Theorem
3.20
. In order to do so, we need to bound the norm of the error
\|u(\cdot,t)-\Psi(\cdot,t)\|_{H_{ul}^{\theta}}
at particular times
t=T_{mid}
and
t=T
. Similarly to the established approaches for the static SH problem (
6
) in e.g.
[
82
, Ch. 10]
, we define a weighted error function
R
via

r(\bar{t})^{\beta}R(x,\bar{t}):=u(x,t)-r(\bar{t})\psi(x,\bar{t}),

(153)

where the exponent
\beta>0
is expected to depend upon the order
n
of the approximation
\Psi
, but left unspecified for now. The first step is to obtain an evolution equation for
R
in terms of
\psi
and
\textrm{Res}(r\psi)
. This equation is expected to differ in the dynamic setting since the variable
r
, which plays the role of the small parameter, depends on time (recall the discussion following the statement of Theorem
3.20
).

Differentiating
u
with respect to
t
and applying the definition (
153
) leads to

\begin{split}\partial_{t}u&=\partial_{t}(r^{\beta}R)+\partial_{t}(r\psi)\\
&=\partial_{t}(r^{\beta}R)-(1+\partial_{x}^{2})^{2}r\psi+r^{3}\bar{v}\psi-r^{2}\psi^{3}+r^{4}\varepsilon\mu(x)-\textrm{Res}(r\psi)\\
&=-(1+\partial_{x}^{2})^{2}(r^{\beta}R+r\psi)+r^{2}\bar{v}(r^{\beta}R+r\psi)-(r^{\beta}R+r\psi)^{3}+r^{4}\bar{\varepsilon}\mu(x).\end{split}

Expanding
\partial_{t}(r^{\beta}R)=r^{\beta}\partial_{t}R+\beta r^{\beta-1}R\partial_{t}r
, changing to the desingularized time
\bar{t}
and rearranging a little, we obtain the following equation for the (weighted) error function
R
:

\partial_{\bar{t}}R=-r^{-2}(1+\partial_{x}^{2})^{2}R+\left(\bar{v}-\beta r^{-1}\partial_{\bar{t}}r-3\psi^{2}\right)R-3r^{\beta-1}R^{2}\psi-r^{2\beta-2}R^{3}+r^{-\beta-2}\textrm{Res}(r\psi).

(154)

Some of the time-dependence in the linear part can be mitigated via the use of an integrating factor. Defining a new function
\tilde{R}
via
R(x,\bar{t})=:\mathrm{e}^{I(\bar{t})}\tilde{R}(x,\bar{t})
, where

I(\bar{t})=-\beta\int_{0}^{\bar{t}}\frac{\partial_{\bar{s}}r(\bar{s})}{r(\bar{s})}d\bar{s},

leads to

\partial_{\bar{t}}\tilde{R}=-r^{-2}(1+\partial_{x}^{2})^{2}\tilde{R}+\bar{v}\tilde{R}-3\psi^{2}\tilde{R}-3r^{\beta-1}\mathrm{e}^{I}\tilde{R}^{2}\psi-r^{2\beta-2}\mathrm{e}^{2I}\tilde{R}^{3}+r^{-\beta-2}\mathrm{e}^{-I}\textrm{Res}(r\psi).

Using the variation of constants formula we obtain

\tilde{R}(\cdot,\bar{t})=\mathrm{e}^{\bar{t}\Lambda}R^{\ast}+\int_{0}^{\bar{t}}\mathrm{e}^{(\bar{t}-\bar{s})\Lambda}\left(F(\cdot,\bar{s})+r(\bar{s})^{-\beta-2}\mathrm{e}^{-I(\bar{s})}\textrm{Res}(r(\bar{s})\psi(\cdot,\bar{s}))\right)d\bar{s},

(155)

where
(\mathrm{e}^{\bar{t}\Lambda})_{\bar{t}\geq 0}
is the evolution family generated by
\Lambda:=-r(\bar{t})^{-2}(1+\partial_{x}^{2})^{2}+\bar{v}(\bar{t})
, the initial condition satisfies
R^{\ast}:=\tilde{R}(\cdot,0)=\tilde{R}(\cdot,0)
, and

F(\cdot,\bar{s}):=-3\psi(\cdot,\bar{s})^{2}\tilde{R}(\cdot,\bar{s})-3r(\bar{s})^{\beta-1}\mathrm{e}^{I(\bar{s})}\tilde{R}(\cdot,\bar{s})^{2}\psi(\cdot,\bar{s})-r(\bar{s})^{2\beta-2}\mathrm{e}^{2I(\bar{s})}\tilde{R}(\cdot,\bar{s})^{3}.

(156)

In order to estimate the norm of (
155
) (and thus also the norm of
R
) in
H_{ul}^{\theta}
, we need to estimate the following:

•

The evolution family
(\mathrm{e}^{\bar{t}\Lambda})_{\bar{t}\geq 0}
;

•

The residual
\textrm{Res}(r(\bar{s})\psi(\cdot,\bar{s}))
;

•

The ‘nonlinear’ terms in
F(\cdot,\bar{s})
.

We derive estimates for each of these terms below. Since the equation for the error in (
154
) is posed in the blown-up space, we will often work in coordinate charts
\mathcal{K}_{l}
.

6.1
Evolution family estimates

We prove the following result.

Lemma 6.1
.

The following estimates for the evolution family are obtained in coordinate charts
\mathcal{K}_{l}
, and hold for every
\theta\geq 0
:

(i)

Chart
\mathcal{K}_{1}
: For each
c_{1}\in(0,1)
there exists a constant
C>0
depending on
\theta
such that

\|\mathrm{e}^{t_{1}\Lambda_{1}}\|_{H_{ul}^{\theta}\to H_{ul}^{\theta}}\leq C\mathrm{e}^{-c_{1}t_{1}}

for all
t_{1}\in[0,T_{1}]
, where
\Lambda_{1}=-r_{1}(t_{1})^{-2}(1+\partial_{x}^{2})^{2}-1
.

(ii)

Chart
\mathcal{K}_{2}
: There exists a constant
C>0
such that

\|\mathrm{e}^{t_{2}\Lambda_{2}}\|_{H_{ul}^{\theta}\to H_{ul}^{\theta}}\leq C

for all
t_{2}\in[0,T_{2}]
, where
\Lambda_{2}=-r_{2}^{-2}(1+\partial_{x}^{2})^{2}+v_{2}(t_{2})
.

(iii)

Chart
\mathcal{K}_{3}
: For each
c_{3}>1
there exists a constant
C>0
depending on
\theta
such that

\|\mathrm{e}^{t_{3}\Lambda_{3}}\|_{H_{ul}^{\theta}\to H_{ul}^{\theta}}\leq C\mathrm{e}^{c_{3}t_{3}}

for all
t_{3}\in[0,T_{3}]
, where
\Lambda_{3}=-r_{3}(t_{3})^{-2}(1+\partial_{x}^{2})^{2}+1
.

Proof.

We begin in global coordinates. The evolution family
(\mathrm{e}^{\bar{t}\Lambda})_{\bar{t}\geq 0}
is induced by the linear equation

\partial_{\bar{t}}\tilde{w}(x,\bar{t})=-\frac{(1+\partial_{x}^{2})^{2}}{r_{1}(\bar{t})^{2}}\tilde{w}(x,\bar{t})+\bar{v}(\bar{s})\tilde{w}(x,\bar{t}),\qquad\tilde{w}(x,0)=w^{\ast}(x),\qquad(\bar{t}\geq 0,\ x\in\mathbb{R}).

Fourier transforming in space leads to a family of linear non-autonomous ODEs

\partial_{\bar{t}}\hat{\tilde{w}}(\xi,\bar{t})=-\frac{(1-\xi^{2})^{2}}{r(\bar{t})^{2}}\hat{\tilde{w}}(\xi,\bar{t})+\bar{v}(\bar{s})\hat{\tilde{w}}(\xi,\bar{t}),\qquad\hat{\tilde{w}}(\xi,0)=\hat{w}^{\ast}(\xi),\qquad(\bar{t}\geq 0,\ \xi\in\mathbb{R}),

with solutions

\hat{\tilde{w}}(\xi,\bar{t})=\exp\left(\int_{0}^{\bar{t}}\left(-\frac{(1-\xi^{2})^{2}}{r(\bar{s})^{2}}+\bar{v}(\bar{s})\right)d\bar{s}\right)\hat{w}^{\ast}(\xi).

Taking the inverse Fourier transform leads to the solution formula

\tilde{w}(\cdot,\bar{t})=\mathscr{F}^{-1}\exp\left(\int_{0}^{\bar{t}}\left(-\frac{(1-\xi^{2})^{2}}{r(\bar{s})^{2}}+\bar{v}(\bar{s})\right)d\bar{s}\right)\mathscr{F}w^{\ast}=:P(\bar{t})w^{\ast}.

Fourier multiplier techniques allow us to study the mapping properties of
\tilde{P}(\bar{t})\colon H^{\theta}_{ul}\to H^{\theta}_{ul},\ w^{\ast}\mapsto P(\bar{t})w^{\ast}
via its symbol

\tilde{p}(\xi,\bar{t}):=\exp\left(\int_{0}^{\bar{t}}\left(-\frac{(1-\xi^{2})^{2}}{r(\bar{s})^{2}}+\bar{v}(\bar{s})\right)d\bar{s}\right).

In particular, Lemma
A.2
shows that it suffices to derive estimates for the
C^{2}_{b}
-norm of
\tilde{p}(\cdot,\bar{t})
. These can be estimated directly using the above and

\begin{split}\partial_{\xi}\tilde{p}(\xi,\bar{t})&=4\xi(1-\xi^{2})\left(\int_{0}^{\bar{t}}\frac{1}{r(\bar{s})^{2}}d\bar{s}\right)\tilde{p}(\xi,\bar{t}),\\
\partial_{\xi}^{2}\tilde{p}(\xi,\bar{t})&=\left(4-12\xi^{2}+16\xi^{2}(1-\xi^{2})^{2}\right)\left(\int_{0}^{\bar{t}}\frac{1}{r(\bar{s})^{2}}d\bar{s}\right)^{2}\tilde{p}(\xi,\bar{t}).\end{split}

In order to estimate
\tilde{p}(\xi,\bar{t})
and the integral terms, we work in charts
\mathcal{K}_{l}
.

In
\mathcal{K}_{1}
we have that
r(\bar{t})=r_{1}(t_{1})=\rho_{in}^{1/2}(1-2\varepsilon_{1}^{\ast}t_{1})^{1/4}
and
\bar{v}(\bar{t})=-1
for all
t_{1}\in[0,T_{1}]
; recall Lemma
5.2
. There are algebraic growth rates in time due to the fact that

\int_{0}^{t_{1}}\frac{1}{r_{1}(s_{1})^{2}}ds_{1}=\rho_{in}^{-1}{\varepsilon_{1}^{\ast}}^{-1}(1-\sqrt{1-2\varepsilon_{1}^{\ast}t_{1}}),

but these are compensated by the exponential decay in the expression for
\tilde{p}(\xi,\bar{t})
, due to the fact that
\bar{v}=-1
in chart
\mathcal{K}_{1}
. In particular, letting
\tilde{p}_{1}
denote the representation of
\tilde{p}
in
\mathcal{K}_{1}
, we find that for each
c_{1}\in(0,1)
there is a constant
C>0
depending on
\theta
such that

\|\tilde{p}_{1}(\cdot,t_{1})\|_{C_{b}^{2}}\leq C\mathrm{e}^{-c_{1}t_{1}},

for all
t_{1}\in[0,T_{1}]
. Lemma
A.2
implies the corresponding result in
H_{ul}^{\theta}
, thereby proving Assertion (i).

In
\mathcal{K}_{2}
we have
r(\bar{t})=r_{2}
and
\bar{v}(\bar{t})=v_{2}(t_{2})=-\zeta^{-1/2}+t_{2}
for all
t_{2}\in[0,T_{2}]
; recall Lemma
5.13
. A direct calculation shows that

\|\tilde{p}_{2}(\cdot,t_{2})\|_{C_{b}^{2}}\leq C\frac{t_{2}^{2}}{r_{2}^{4}}\mathrm{e}^{-(1-\xi^{2})^{2}t_{2}/r_{2}^{2}}

for all
t_{2}\in[0,T_{2}]
. Thus there is exponential decay for all wavenumbers
\xi
bounded away from the critical modes
\xi\pm 1
. It turns out that the unwanted algebraic growth close to
\xi\pm 1
can be mitigated using
mode filters
. Due to the similarity with pre-existing arguments in e.g.
[
65
,
73
,
74
,
82
]
, we omit the details, noting simply that close for
\xi=\pm 1\pm r_{2}\bar{\xi}
close to
\xi=\pm 1
one has
-(1-\xi^{2})^{2}=-4(r_{2}\bar{\xi})^{2}+O((r_{2}\bar{\xi})^{4})
so that

\exp\left(-\int_{0}^{t_{2}}\left(4\bar{\xi}^{2}+O((r_{2}\bar{\xi})^{2}\right)ds_{2}\right)=\exp\left(-4\bar{\xi}^{2}t_{2}+O((r_{2}\bar{\xi})^{2}t_{2})\right),

which is bounded for all
t_{2}\in[0,T_{2}]
. This leads to a
C^{2}_{b}
norm which is growing at a rate proportional to
t_{2}^{2}
, which is finite on
[0,T_{2}]
. Applying Lemma
A.2
yields the
H_{ul}^{\theta}
estimate in Assertion (ii).

In
\mathcal{K}_{3}
we have
r(\bar{t})=r_{3}(t_{3})=r_{3}^{\ast}(1+2\zeta t_{3})^{1/4}
and
\bar{v}(\bar{t})=1
for all
t_{3}\in[0,T_{3}]
; recall Lemma
5.19
. In this case we also identify algebraic growth rates in time, however these are dominated by the exponential growth due to the fact that
\bar{v}=1
in chart
\mathcal{K}_{3}
. In particular, for each
c_{3}>1
there is a
c_{3}
such that

\|\tilde{p}_{3}(\xi,t_{3})\|_{C_{b}^{2}}\leq C\mathrm{e}^{c_{3}t_{3}}

for all
t_{3}>0
. The bound in Assertion (iii) follows after applying Lemma
A.2
.
∎

6.2
Residual estimates

In the following we require extra regularity in order to control higher order contributions to the residual because of the reasons given in Remark
4.5
. In order to keep track of this extra regularity we use

\theta_{j,k}:=1+3(j-k+1)+\theta=\begin{cases}\theta_{A}(j)=1+3j+\theta,&k=1,\\
3(j-k+1)+\theta,&k=2,\ldots,j-1,\\
4+\theta,&k=j,\end{cases}

instead of the earlier definition in (
84
). We obtain the following result.

Lemma 6.2
.

Let
\theta_{A}=1+3(n-3)+\theta
and
\theta\geq 1
. Then

\|\textup{Res}(r_{l}(t_{l})\psi_{l}(\cdot,t_{l}))\|_{H_{ul}^{\theta}}\leq Cr_{l}(t_{l})^{n}\left(\delta_{n,4}\max_{m\in I_{N}}|\nu_{m}|+\sup_{m\in I_{N},j=1,\ldots,\tilde{\alpha}(m)}\|A_{mj,l}(\cdot,t_{l})\|_{H_{ul}^{\alpha(m)+j,N}}\right),

(157)

for
t_{l}\in[0,T_{l}]
and
l=1,2
, where

\delta_{n,4}:=\begin{cases}1,&n=4,\\
0,&n\geq 4.\end{cases}

If additionally
\mu(x)\equiv 0
and the conditions of Proposition
5.25
are satisfied, then

\|\textup{Res}(r_{l}(t_{l})\psi_{l}(\cdot,t_{l}))\|_{H_{ul}^{\theta}}\leq Cr_{l}(t_{l})^{n}\sup_{k=1,\ldots,N-2}\|A_{1k,l}(\cdot,t_{l})\|_{H_{ul}^{\theta_{k,N-2}}},

(158)

for all
t_{1}\in[0,T_{l}]
and
l=1,2,3
.

Proof.

We begin in global coordinates. It follows from the formal construction for
\Psi=r\psi
in Section
4
that

\begin{split}\textrm{Res}(r\psi)&=\delta_{n,4}r^{4}\bar{\varepsilon}\sum_{m\in I_{N}}\nu_{m}\mathrm{e}^{imx}\\
&+\sum_{m\in I_{N}}\sum_{j=1}^{\tilde{\alpha}(m)}r^{\alpha(m)+j}\big(r\mathcal{L}_{m}^{(1)}+r^{2}\tilde{\mathcal{L}_{m}^{(2)}}+r^{3}\mathcal{L}_{m}^{(3)}+r^{4}\mathcal{L}_{m}^{(4)}\big)A_{mj}\mathrm{e}^{imx}-\sum_{m\in I_{3N}\setminus I_{N}}b_{m}\mathrm{e}^{imx}.\end{split}

(159)

The first sum is easily controlled. The second sum has terms which vanish up to and including
O(r^{N})
, however we need to control the higher order terms at
O(r^{N+1})=O(r^{n})
,
O(r^{n+1})
,
O(r^{n+2})
and
O(r^{n+3})
. Since
\tilde{\alpha}(m)\leq N-1
for all
m\in I_{N}
, terms involving
r\mathcal{L}_{m}^{(1)}
must be
O(r^{n})
with
\alpha(m)+j=N
. It follows that

\sum_{m\in I_{N}}\sum_{j=1}^{\tilde{\alpha}(m)}r^{\alpha(m)+j}r\mathcal{L}_{m}^{(1)}A_{mj}\mathrm{e}^{imx}=r^{n}\mathcal{L}_{m}^{(1)}\left(A_{0N-1}+A_{2N-1}\mathrm{e}^{2ix}+A_{3N-2}\mathrm{e}^{3ix}+\cdots+A_{N1}\mathrm{e}^{Nix}\right)+c.c.,

the norm of which is bounded by
Cr^{n}
since the non-critical modulation functions
A_{mj}
with
|m|\neq 1
inside the term in parentheses are bounded according to Lemmas
5.9
,
5.17
and
5.24
. More precisely, we have that

\bigg\|x\mapsto\sum_{m\in I_{N}}\sum_{j=1}^{\tilde{\alpha}(m)}r^{\alpha(m)+j}r\mathcal{L}_{m}^{(1)}A_{mj}\mathrm{e}^{imx}\bigg\|_{H_{ul}^{\theta}}\leq Cr^{n}\sup_{\{(m,j):\alpha(m)+j=N\}}\|A_{mj}(\cdot,\bar{t})\|_{H_{ul}^{\theta+2}},

where we require two extra degrees of regularity (one is needed to apply Lemma
A.1
, the other is needed to control the
\partial_{\bar{x}}
derivative in
\mathcal{L}_{m}^{(1)}
). Similar arguments relying on the bounds obtained for the modulation functions
A_{mj}
in Section
5
show that

\begin{split}\bigg\|x\mapsto\sum_{m\in I_{N}}\sum_{j=1}^{\tilde{\alpha}(m)}r^{\alpha(m)+j}r^{2}\tilde{\mathcal{L}}_{m}^{(2)}A_{mj}\mathrm{e}^{imx}\bigg\|_{H_{ul}^{\theta}}&\leq Cr^{n}\sup_{\{(m,j):\alpha(m)+j=N-1\}}\|A_{mj}(\cdot,\bar{t})\|_{H_{ul}^{\theta+3}}\\
&+Cr^{n+1}\sup_{\{(m,j):\alpha(m)+j=N\}}\|A_{mj}(\cdot,\bar{t})\|_{H_{ul}^{\theta+3}},\\
\bigg\|x\mapsto\sum_{m\in I_{N}}\sum_{j=1}^{\tilde{\alpha}(m)}r^{\alpha(m)+j}r^{3}\mathcal{L}_{m}^{(3)}A_{mj}\mathrm{e}^{imx}\bigg\|_{H_{ul}^{\theta}}&\leq Cr^{n}\sup_{\{(m,j):\alpha(m)+j=N-2\}}\|A_{mj}(\cdot,\bar{t})\|_{H_{ul}^{\theta+4}}\\
&+Cr^{n+1}\sup_{\{(m,j):\alpha(m)+j=N-1\}}\|A_{mj}(\cdot,\bar{t})\|_{H_{ul}^{\theta+4}}\\
&+Cr^{n+2}\sup_{\{(m,j):\alpha(m)+j=N\}}\|A_{mj}(\cdot,\bar{t})\|_{H_{ul}^{\theta+4}},\\
\bigg\|x\mapsto\sum_{m\in I_{N}}\sum_{j=1}^{\tilde{\alpha}(m)}r^{\alpha(m)+j}r^{4}\mathcal{L}_{m}^{(4)}A_{mj}\mathrm{e}^{imx}\bigg\|_{H_{ul}^{\theta}}&\leq Cr^{n}\sup_{\{(m,j):\alpha(m)+j=N-3\}}\|A_{mj}(\cdot,\bar{t})\|_{H_{ul}^{\theta+5}}\\
&+Cr^{n+1}\sup_{\{(m,j):\alpha(m)+j=N-2\}}\|A_{mj}(\cdot,\bar{t})\|_{H_{ul}^{\theta+5}}\\
&+Cr^{n+2}\sup_{\{(m,j):\alpha(m)+j=N-1\}}\|A_{mj}(\cdot,\bar{t})\|_{H_{ul}^{\theta+5}}\\
&+Cr^{n+3}\sup_{\{(m,j):\alpha(m)+j=N\}}\|A_{mj}(\cdot,\bar{t})\|_{H_{ul}^{\theta+5}},\end{split}

(160)

from which it follows that

\begin{split}\bigg\|x\mapsto\sum_{m\in I_{N}}\sum_{j=1}^{\tilde{\alpha}(m)}r^{\alpha(m)+j}\big(r\mathcal{L}_{m}^{(1)}+r^{2}\tilde{\mathcal{L}_{m}^{(2)}}+r^{3}\mathcal{L}_{m}^{(3)}&+r^{4}\mathcal{L}_{m}^{(4)}\big)A_{mj}\mathrm{e}^{imx}\bigg\|_{H_{ul}^{\theta}}\leq\\
&Cr^{n}\sup_{\{(m,j):N-3\leq\alpha(m)+j\leq N\}}\|A_{mj}(\cdot,\bar{t})\|_{H_{ul}^{\theta+5}}.\end{split}

It remains to consider the third sum in (
159
). Recall that for each
m\in I_{3N}\setminus I_{N}
,
b_{m}
is a linear combination of terms of the form

r^{\alpha(m_{1})+\alpha(m_{2})+\alpha(m_{3})+j_{1}+j_{2}+j_{3}}A_{m_{1}j_{1}}A_{m_{2}j_{2}}A_{m_{3}j_{3}},

with
m_{i}\in I_{N}
and
j_{i}\in\{1,\ldots,\tilde{\alpha}(m_{i})\}
for each
i=1,2,3
. Moreover,

\alpha(m_{1})+\alpha(m_{2})+\alpha(m_{3})+j_{1}+j_{2}+j_{3}=\alpha(m)+j\geq|m|-1+1\geq n,

since
m=m_{1}+m_{2}+m_{3}\in I_{3N}\setminus I_{N}
and
j\geq 1
. For
l=1,2
and additionally
l=3
if
\mu(x)\equiv 0
and the conditions of Proposition
5.25
are satisfied, the results in Section
5
show that all modulation functions satisfy
A_{mj,l}(\cdot,t_{l})\in H_{ul}^{\theta+1}
, for all
t_{l}\in[0,T_{l}]
; we refer again to Lemmas
5.9
,
5.17
and
5.24
. Using Lemmas
A.1
and
A.3
we therefore obtain

\begin{split}\bigg\|x\mapsto\sum_{m\in I_{3N}\setminus I_{N}}b_{m}(r_{l}(t_{l})x,t_{l})\mathrm{e}^{imx}\bigg\|_{H_{ul}^{\theta}}&\leq\sum_{m\in I_{3N}\setminus I_{N}}\|x\mapsto b_{m}(r_{l}(t_{l})x,t_{l})\mathrm{e}^{imx}\|_{H_{ul}^{\theta}}\\
&\leq Cr_{l}(t_{l})^{n}\sup_{m\in I_{N},j\in\{1,\ldots,\tilde{\alpha}(m)\}}\|x\mapsto A_{mj,l}(r_{l}(t_{l})x,t_{l})\mathrm{e}^{imx}\|_{H_{ul}^{\theta}}\\
&\leq Cr_{l}(t_{l})^{n}\sup_{m\in I_{N},j\in\{1,\ldots,\tilde{\alpha}(m)\}}\|A_{mj,l}(\cdot,t_{l})\|_{H_{ul}^{\theta+1}},\end{split}

for all
t_{l}\in[0,T_{l}]
,
l=1,2
and also for
l=3
if
\mu(x)\equiv 0
and the conditions of Proposition
5.25
are satisfied.

Taking a triangle inequality in (
159
) and using the bounds for the second and third sums derived above yields the bound in (
157
). If
\mu(x)\equiv 0
, then
\nu_{m}=0
for all
m\in I_{N}
in (
157
). It also follows from the proof of Lemmas
5.7
and
5.9
(see equations (
89
) and (
95
) in particular) that

\|A_{mj,l}(\cdot,t_{l})\|_{H_{ul}^{\theta_{\alpha(m)+j,N}}}\leq C\sup_{k=1,\ldots,j-2}\|A_{\pm 1k,l}(\cdot,t_{1})\|_{H_{ul}^{\theta_{k,N-2}}},

for all
t_{l}\in[0,T_{l}]
and
l=1,2,3
. Combining this with (
157
) yields the bound in (
158
).
∎

Remark 6.3
.

In Lemma
6.2
we require
\theta_{A}=1+3(n-3)+\theta
as opposed to lesser requirement that
\theta_{A}=1+3(N-3)+\theta=1+3(n-4)+\theta
, which is all that was needed in order to describe the approximation dynamics in Lemmas
3.14
and
3.16
. Greater regularity is needed to bound the residual because we need to control the norms in (
160
); see also Remark
4.5
.

6.3
Estimates for the nonlinear terms

Let
F_{l}(x,t_{l})
denote the local representation of
F(x,\bar{t})
in chart
\mathcal{K}_{l}
, and define

\tilde{T}_{l}:=\min\left\{T_{l},\sup\left\{t_{l}>0:\|\tilde{R}_{l}(\cdot,t_{l})\|_{H_{ul}^{\theta}}\leq\tilde{M}\right\}\right\}

where
\tilde{M}>M\geq\|R^{\ast}\|_{H_{ul}^{\theta}}
, for each
l=1,2,3
. We prove the following result.

Lemma 6.4
.

Let
\theta_{A}=1+3(N-3)+\theta
where
\theta>1/2
. We obtain the following estimates in charts
\mathcal{K}_{l}
:

(i)

In chart
\mathcal{K}_{1}
we have

\begin{split}\|F_{1}(\cdot,s_{1})\|_{H_{ul}^{\theta}}\leq C\bigg(\|\psi_{1}(\cdot,s_{1})\|_{H_{ul}^{\theta}}^{2}+\frac{\rho_{in}^{(\beta-1)/2}}{(1-2\varepsilon_{1}^{\ast}s_{1})^{1/4}}\|\tilde{R}_{1}(\cdot,s_{1})\|_{H_{ul}^{\theta}}&\|\psi_{1}(\cdot,s_{1})\|_{H_{ul}^{\theta}}\\
+\frac{\rho_{in}^{\beta-1}}{(1-2\varepsilon_{1}^{\ast}s_{1})^{1/2}}&\|\tilde{R}_{1}(\cdot,s_{1})\|_{H_{ul}^{\theta}}^{2}\bigg)\|\tilde{R}_{1}(\cdot,s_{1})\|_{H_{ul}^{\theta}},\end{split}

for all
t_{1}\in[0,\tilde{T}_{1}]
.

(ii)

In chart
\mathcal{K}_{2}
we have

\begin{split}\|F_{2}(\cdot,s_{2})\|_{H_{ul}^{\theta}}\leq C\bigg(\|\psi_{2}(\cdot,s_{2})\|_{H_{ul}^{\theta}}^{2}+r_{2}^{\beta-1}\|\tilde{R}_{2}(\cdot,s_{2})\|_{H_{ul}^{\theta}}&\|\psi_{2}(\cdot,s_{2})\|_{H_{ul}^{\theta}}\\
+r_{2}^{2\beta-2}&\|\tilde{R}_{2}(\cdot,s_{2})\|_{H_{ul}^{\theta}}^{2}\bigg)\|\tilde{R}_{2}(\cdot,s_{2})\|_{H_{ul}^{\theta}},\end{split}

for all
t_{2}\in[0,\tilde{T}_{2}]
.

(iii)

If
\mu(x)\equiv 0
and the conditions of Proposition
5.25
are satisfied then in chart
\mathcal{K}_{3}
we have

\begin{split}\|F_{3}(\cdot,s_{3})\|_{H_{ul}^{\theta}}\leq C\bigg(\|\psi_{3}(\cdot,s_{3})\|_{H_{ul}^{\theta}}^{2}+\frac{{r_{3}^{\ast}}^{\beta-1}}{(1+2\zeta s_{3})^{1/4}}\|\tilde{R}_{3}(\cdot,s_{3})\|_{H_{ul}^{\theta}}&\|\psi_{3}(\cdot,s_{3})\|_{H_{ul}^{\theta}}\\
+\frac{{r_{3}^{\ast}}^{2\beta-2}}{(1+2\zeta s_{3})^{1/2}}&\|\tilde{R}_{3}(\cdot,s_{3})\|_{H_{ul}^{\theta}}^{2}\bigg)\|\tilde{R}_{3}(\cdot,s_{3})\|_{H_{ul}^{\theta}},\end{split}

for all
t_{3}\in[0,\tilde{T}_{3}]
.

Proof.

By Propositions
5.11
,
5.18
and
5.25
we have that
\psi_{l}(\cdot,t_{l})\in H_{ul}^{\theta}
for all
t_{l}\in[0,\tilde{T}_{l}]
(although only if
\mu(x)\equiv 0
and the conditions of Proposition
5.25
are satisfied in case
l=3
). Since
H_{ul}^{\theta}
is closed under multiplication and
\tilde{R}_{l}(\cdot,t_{l})\in H_{ul}^{\theta}
for all
t_{l}\in[0,\tilde{T}_{l}]
by the definition of
\tilde{T}_{l}
, it follows that for any fixed integers
n_{1},n_{2}\geq 1
there is a constant
C>0
such that

\|\psi_{l}(\cdot,s_{l})^{n_{1}}\tilde{R}_{l}(\cdot,s_{l})^{n_{2}}\|_{H_{ul}^{\theta}}\leq C\|\psi_{l}(\cdot,s_{l})\|_{H_{ul}^{\theta}}^{n_{1}}\|\tilde{R}_{l}(\cdot,s_{l})\|_{H_{ul}^{\theta}}^{n_{2}},\qquad t_{l}\in[0,\tilde{T}_{l}].

Thus we obtain the following after taking the norm of (
156
):

\begin{split}\|F_{l}(\cdot,s_{l})\|_{H_{ul}^{\theta}}\leq C\bigg(\|\psi_{l}(\cdot,s_{l})\|_{H_{ul}^{\theta}}^{2}+r_{l}(s_{l})^{\beta-1}\mathrm{e}^{I_{l}(s_{l})}\|\tilde{R}_{l}(\cdot,s_{l})\|_{H_{ul}^{\theta}}&\|\psi_{l}(\cdot,s_{l})\|_{H_{ul}^{\theta}}\\
+r_{l}(s_{l})^{2\beta-2}\mathrm{e}^{2I_{l}(s_{l})}&\|\tilde{R}_{l}(\cdot,s_{l})\|_{H_{ul}^{\theta}}^{2}\bigg)\|\tilde{R}_{l}(\cdot,s_{l})\|_{H_{ul}^{\theta}},\end{split}

where
I_{l}(s_{l})
denotes the local representation of
I(\bar{s})
in chart
\mathcal{K}_{l}
. The expressions in Assertions (i), (ii) and (iii) follow from the explicit solution formulae for
r_{l}(t_{l})
(see Lemmas
5.2
,
5.19
and recall that
r_{2}(t_{2})=r_{2}
) together with

\mathrm{e}^{I_{1}(s_{1})}=\frac{1}{(1-2\varepsilon_{1}^{\ast}s_{1})^{\beta/4}},\qquad\mathrm{e}^{I_{2}(s_{2})}=1,\qquad\mathrm{e}^{I_{3}(s_{3})}=\frac{1}{(1+2\zeta s_{3})^{\beta/4}}.

∎

6.4
Proof of Theorem
3.20

In this section we use the estimates derived in Sections
6.1
,
6.2
and
6.3
above to prove Theorem
3.20
. We begin by deriving estimates for the (weighted) error function
R_{l}
in local coordinate charts
\mathcal{K}_{l}
.

6.4.1
Error estimates in charts

Using (
155
) and the results obtained above we can estimate the error in each chart
\mathcal{K}_{l}
.

Proposition 6.5
.

Let
\theta_{A}=1+3(n-3)+\theta
where
\theta\geq 1
, and
\beta=n-2
. Then for
l=1,2
we have that
R_{l}(\cdot,t_{l})\in H_{ul}^{\theta}
for all
t_{l}\in[0,T_{l}]
, with

\|R_{1}(\cdot,T_{1})\|_{H_{ul}^{\theta}}\leq C\left(1+\|R_{1}^{\ast}\|_{H_{ul}^{\theta}}\mathrm{e}^{-\tilde{c}_{1}/2\varepsilon_{1}^{\ast}}\right),\qquad\|R_{2}(\cdot,T_{2})\|_{H_{ul}^{\theta}}\leq C\left(1+\|R_{2}^{\ast}\|_{H_{ul}^{\theta}}\right),

where
\tilde{c}_{1}=c_{1}-2C\tilde{C}(\tilde{K},\tilde{M})\in(0,1)
can be chosen arbitrarily close to
1
by fixing
K,M>0
sufficiently small. If additionally
\mu(x)\equiv 0
then the following assertions are true:

(i)

In chart
\mathcal{K}_{1}
we have

\|R_{1}(\cdot,T_{1})\|_{H_{ul}^{\theta}}\leq C\left(\|R_{1}^{\ast}\|_{H_{ul}^{\theta}}+\max_{k=1,\ldots,N-2}\|A_{k,1}^{\ast}\|_{H_{ul}^{\theta_{k,N-2}}}\right)\mathrm{e}^{-\kappa/2\varepsilon_{1}^{\ast}}.

(ii)

In chart
\mathcal{K}_{2}
we have

\|R_{2}(\cdot,T_{2})\|_{H_{ul}^{\theta}}\leq C\left(\|R_{2}^{\ast}\|_{H_{ul}^{\theta}}+\max_{k=1,\ldots,N-2}\|A_{k,2}^{\ast}\|_{H_{ul}^{\theta_{k,N-2}}}\right).

(iii)

If the conditions of Proposition
5.25
are satisfied, in chart
\mathcal{K}_{3}
we have that
R_{3}(\cdot,t_{3})\in H_{ul}^{\theta}
for all
t_{3}\in[0,T_{3}]
and

\|R_{3}(\cdot,T_{3})\|_{H_{ul}^{\theta}}\leq C{r_{3}^{\ast}}^{n-2}\left(\|R_{3}^{\ast}\|_{H_{ul}^{\theta}}+\mathrm{e}^{-(\tilde{\sigma}_{0}-\tilde{\sigma})\rho_{out}^{2}/2\zeta{r_{3}^{\ast}}^{4}}\right)\mathrm{e}^{\tilde{c}_{3}\rho_{out}^{2}/2\zeta{r_{3}^{\ast}}^{4}},

where
\tilde{c}_{3}:=c_{3}+2C\tilde{C}(\tilde{K},\tilde{M})>1
can be set arbitrarily close to
1
by fixing sufficiently small
K,M>0
.

Proof.

In chart
\mathcal{K}_{1}
we have

\begin{split}\tilde{R}_{1}(\cdot,t_{1})=\mathrm{e}^{t_{1}\Lambda_{1}}R_{1}^{\ast}&+\int_{0}^{t_{1}}\mathrm{e}^{(t_{1}-s_{1})\Lambda_{1}}F_{1}(\cdot,s_{1})ds_{1}\\
&+\int_{0}^{t_{1}}\mathrm{e}^{(t_{1}-s_{1})\Lambda_{1}}r_{1}(s_{1})^{-\beta-2}\mathrm{e}^{-I_{1}(s_{1})}\textup{Res}(r_{1}(s_{1})\psi_{1}(\cdot,s_{1}))ds_{1},\end{split}

for
t_{1}\in[0,\tilde{T}_{1}]
. Using the bound for the evolution family in Lemma
6.1
, we obtain

\begin{split}\|\tilde{R}_{1}(\cdot,t_{1})\|_{H_{ul}^{\theta}}\leq C\mathrm{e}^{-c_{1}t_{1}}\|R_{1}^{\ast}\|_{H_{ul}^{\theta}}&+C\int_{0}^{t_{1}}\mathrm{e}^{-c_{1}(t_{1}-s_{1})}\|F_{1}(\cdot,s_{1})\|_{H_{ul}^{\theta}}ds_{1}\\
+\ C\int_{0}^{t_{1}}\mathrm{e}^{-c_{1}(t_{1}-s_{1})}&r_{1}(s_{1})^{-\beta-2}\mathrm{e}^{-I_{1}(s_{1})}\|\textup{Res}(r_{1}(s_{1})\psi_{1}(\cdot,s_{1}))\|_{H_{ul}^{\theta}}ds_{1},\end{split}

(161)

for all
t_{1}\in[0,\tilde{T}_{1}]
. The right-most integral can be bounded using Lemma
6.2
and the fact that
r_{1}(s_{1})^{-\beta-2}\mathrm{e}^{-I_{1}(s_{1})}=1/(\rho_{in}^{\beta/2}r_{1}(s_{1})^{2})
. We obtain

\begin{split}\int_{0}^{t_{1}}\mathrm{e}^{-c_{1}(t_{1}-s_{1})}r_{1}(s_{1})^{-\beta-2}\mathrm{e}^{-I_{1}(s_{1})}&\|\textup{Res}(r_{1}(s_{1})\psi_{1}(\cdot,s_{1}))\|_{H_{ul}^{\theta}}ds_{1}\\
\leq\frac{C}{\rho_{in}^{\beta/2}}\int_{0}^{t_{1}}\mathrm{e}^{-c_{1}(t_{1}-s_{1})}&r_{1}(s_{1})^{n-2}\left(\delta_{n,4}\max_{m\in I_{N}}|\nu_{m}|+\sup_{m,j}\|A_{mj,1}(\cdot,s_{1})\|_{H_{ul}^{\theta_{\alpha(m)+j,N}}}\right)ds_{1},\end{split}

(162)

for all
t_{1}\in[0,T_{1}]
, where the supremum is taken with respect to modulation functions with
m\in I_{N}
and
j\in\{1,\ldots,\tilde{\alpha}(m)\}
. By Lemma
5.9
, all such modulation functions in chart
\mathcal{K}_{1}
satisfy
A_{mj,1}(\cdot,s_{1})\in H_{ul}^{\theta+1}
for all
s_{1}\in[0,T_{1}]
. It follows that

\begin{split}\frac{C}{\rho_{in}^{\beta/2}}\int_{0}^{t_{1}}\mathrm{e}^{-c_{1}(t_{1}-s_{1})}r_{1}(s_{1})^{n-2}\left(\delta_{n,4}\max_{m\in I_{N}}|\nu_{m}|+\sup_{m,j}\|A_{mj,1}(\cdot,s_{1})\|_{H_{ul}^{\theta_{\alpha(m)+j,N}}}\right)ds_{1}&\leq\\
\frac{C}{\rho_{in}^{\beta/2}}\int_{0}^{t_{1}}\mathrm{e}^{-c_{1}(t_{1}-s_{1})}&r_{1}(s_{1})^{n-2}ds_{1}.\end{split}

The right-most integral can be evaluated directly using the expression for
r_{1}(s_{1})
in Lemma
5.2
together with the integral identity and gamma function properties in Lemma
A.5
. We obtain

\begin{split}\mathrm{e}^{-c_{1}t_{1}}\int_{0}^{t_{1}}\mathrm{e}^{c_{1}s_{1}}r_{1}(s_{1})^{n-2}ds_{1}&=C\rho_{in}^{n-2}\mathrm{e}^{-c_{1}(t_{1}-1/2\varepsilon_{1}^{\ast})}{\varepsilon_{1}^{\ast}}^{(n-2)/4}\Gamma\left(\frac{n+2}{4},c_{1}\left(\frac{1}{2\varepsilon_{1}^{\ast}}-s_{1}\right)\right)\bigg|_{0}^{t_{1}}\\
&\leq C\rho_{in}^{n-2}{\varepsilon_{1}^{\ast}}^{(n-2)/4},\end{split}

for all
t_{1}\in[0,T_{1}]
. Combining this with (
161
) leads to

\|\tilde{R}_{1}(\cdot,t_{1})\|_{H_{ul}^{\theta}}\leq C\left(\mathrm{e}^{-c_{1}t_{1}}\|R_{1}^{\ast}\|_{H_{ul}^{\theta}}+\rho_{in}^{n-\beta-2}{\varepsilon_{1}^{\ast}}^{(n-2)/4}\right)+C\int_{0}^{t_{1}}\mathrm{e}^{-c_{1}(t_{1}-s_{1})}\|F_{1}(\cdot,s_{1})\|_{H_{ul}^{\theta}}ds_{1},

for
t_{1}\in[0,\tilde{T}_{1}]
(at this point the estimates in Lemma
6.4
are only shown to hold for
t_{1}\in[0,\tilde{T}_{1}]
). For our present purposes it suffices to note that by Lemma
6.4
Assertion (i) we have

\|F_{1}(\cdot,s_{1})\|_{H_{ul}^{\theta}}\leq\tilde{C}(\tilde{M},\tilde{K})\|\tilde{R}_{1}(\cdot,s_{1})\|_{H_{ul}^{\theta}}

for all
s_{1}\in[0,\tilde{T}_{1}]
, where the constant
\tilde{C}(\tilde{K},\tilde{M})>0
can be chosen arbitrarily small by choosing
K,M>0
sufficiently small (we only require that
\tilde{K}>K
and
\tilde{M}>M
). Thus

\|\tilde{R}_{1}(\cdot,t_{1})\|_{H_{ul}^{\theta}}\leq C\left(\mathrm{e}^{-c_{1}t_{1}}\|R_{1}^{\ast}\|_{H_{ul}^{\theta}}+\rho_{in}^{n-\beta-2}{\varepsilon_{1}^{\ast}}^{(n-2)/4}\right)+C\tilde{C}(\tilde{K},\tilde{M})\int_{0}^{t_{1}}\mathrm{e}^{-c_{1}(t_{1}-s_{1})}\|\tilde{R}_{1}(\cdot,s_{1})\|_{H_{ul}^{\theta}}ds_{1},

for all
t_{1}\in[0,\tilde{T}_{1}]
. Applying the Grönwall inequality in Lemma
A.4
yields

\begin{split}\|\tilde{R}_{1}(\cdot,t_{1})\|_{H_{ul}^{\theta}}&\leq C\left(\rho_{in}^{n-\beta-2}{\varepsilon_{1}^{\ast}}^{(n-2)/4}+\|R_{1}^{\ast}\|_{H_{ul}^{\theta}}\right)\mathrm{e}^{-(c_{1}-2C\tilde{C}(\tilde{K},\tilde{M}))t_{1}}\\
&+{\varepsilon_{1}^{\ast}}^{(n-2)/4}\rho_{in}^{n-\beta-2}\int_{0}^{t_{1}}\mathrm{e}^{-(c_{1}-2C\tilde{C}(\tilde{K},\tilde{M}))(t_{1}-s_{1})}ds_{1}\\
&=C\left(\rho_{in}^{n-\beta-2}{\varepsilon_{1}^{\ast}}^{(n-2)/4}+\|R_{1}^{\ast}\|_{H_{ul}^{\theta}}\right)\mathrm{e}^{-(c_{1}-2\tilde{C})t_{1}}\\
&+C\rho_{in}^{n-\beta-2}{\varepsilon_{1}^{\ast}}^{(n-2)/4}\left(\frac{1-\mathrm{e}^{-(c_{1}-2\tilde{C})t_{1}}}{c_{1}-2C\tilde{C}(\tilde{K},\tilde{M})}\right),\end{split}

for all
t_{1}\in[0,\tilde{T}_{1}]
. Choosing
K,M>0
sufficiently small allows us to choose
\tilde{C}(\tilde{K},\tilde{M})
such that
c_{1}-2C\tilde{C}(\tilde{K},\tilde{M})\in(0,1)
. In this case the right-hand side is finite for all
t_{1}\in[0,T_{1}]
, such that
\tilde{T}_{1}=T_{1}
. Multiplying through by the integrating factor yields

\begin{split}\|R_{1}(\cdot,t_{1})\|_{H_{ul}^{\theta}}&=\mathrm{e}^{I_{1}(t_{1})}\|\tilde{R}_{1}(\cdot,t_{1})\|_{H_{ul}^{\theta}}\\
&\leq\frac{C}{(1-2\varepsilon_{1}^{\ast}t_{1})^{\beta/4}}\left(\|R_{1}^{\ast}\|_{H_{ul}^{\theta}}\mathrm{e}^{-\tilde{c}_{1}t_{1}}+\rho_{in}^{n-\beta-2}{\varepsilon_{1}^{\ast}}^{(n-2)/4}\right),\end{split}

(163)

where
\tilde{c}_{1}=c_{1}-2C\tilde{C}(\tilde{K},\tilde{M})\in(0,1)
. Since
(1-2\varepsilon_{1}^{\ast}t_{1})^{-\beta/4}\in[1,(\zeta/\varepsilon_{1}^{\ast})^{\beta/4}]
for
t_{1}\in[0,T_{1}]
, (
163
) is bounded above by a constant if we set
\beta=n-2
, as required. Subsequent evaluation at
t_{1}=T_{1}
yields the desired bound.

In order get a better bound when
\mu(x)\equiv 0
, we start again with (
162
) except with
\nu_{m}=0
for all
m\in I_{N}
. We use the improved bound (
6.2
) in Lemma
6.2
, which applies when
\mu(x)\equiv 0
.
Substituting this for right-most bound into (
162
) leads to the upper bound

\begin{split}C\mathrm{e}^{-\tilde{c}t_{1}}\max_{k=1,\ldots,N-2}\|A_{k,1}^{\ast}\|_{H_{ul}^{\theta_{k,N-2}}}\int_{0}^{t_{1}}\mathrm{e}^{(c_{1}-\tilde{c})s_{1}}(1-2\varepsilon_{1}^{\ast}s_{1})^{(n-3)/4}ds_{1}&\leq\\
C\mathrm{e}^{-\tilde{c}t_{1}}&\max_{k=1,\ldots,N-2}\|A_{k,1}^{\ast}\|_{H_{ul}^{\theta_{k,N-2}}},\end{split}

which we obtain by using
(1-2\varepsilon_{1}^{\ast}s_{1})^{(n-3)/4}\leq 1
and setting
c_{1}=\tilde{c}
. Thus we have shown that

\begin{split}\|\tilde{R}_{1}(\cdot,t_{1})\|_{H_{ul}^{\theta}}\leq C\mathrm{e}^{-c_{1}t_{1}}&\left(\|R_{1}^{\ast}\|_{H_{ul}^{\theta}}+\max_{k=1,\ldots,N-2}\|A_{k,1}^{\ast}\|_{H_{ul}^{\theta_{k,N-2}}}\right)\\
&\qquad\qquad+C\tilde{C}(\tilde{K},\tilde{M})\int_{0}^{t_{1}}\mathrm{e}^{-c_{1}(t_{1}-s_{1})}\|\tilde{R}_{1}(\cdot,s_{1})\|_{H_{ul}^{\theta}}ds_{1}.\end{split}

Applying the Grönwall inequality in Lemma
A.4
gives

\|\tilde{R}_{1}(\cdot,t_{1})\|_{H_{ul}^{\theta}}\leq C\left(\|R_{1}^{\ast}\|_{H_{ul}^{\theta}}+\max_{k=1,\ldots,N-2}\|A_{k,1}^{\ast}\|_{H_{ul}^{\theta_{k,N-2}}}\right)\mathrm{e}^{-(c_{1}-2C\tilde{C}(\tilde{K},\tilde{M}))t_{1}}.

Multiplying through by the integrating factor and evaluating at
t_{1}=T_{1}
we obtain

\|R_{1}(\cdot,T_{1})\|_{H_{ul}^{\theta}}\leq C\left(\|R_{1}^{\ast}\|_{H_{ul}^{\theta}}+\max_{k=1,\ldots,N-2}\|A_{k,1}^{\ast}\|_{H_{ul}^{\theta_{k,N-2}}}\right)\mathrm{e}^{-\kappa/2\varepsilon_{1}^{\ast}},

as required.

In chart
\mathcal{K}_{2}
we have

R_{2}(\cdot,t_{2})=\mathrm{e}^{t_{2}\Lambda_{2}}R_{2}^{\ast}+\int_{0}^{t_{2}}\mathrm{e}^{(t_{2}-s_{2})\Lambda_{2}}F_{2}(\cdot,s_{2})ds_{2}\\
+\int_{0}^{t_{2}}\mathrm{e}^{(t_{2}-s_{2})\Lambda_{2}}r_{2}^{-\beta-2}\textup{Res}(r_{2}\psi_{2}(\cdot,s_{2}))ds_{2},

(164)

for
t_{2}\in[0,\tilde{T}_{2}]
(recall that
\mathrm{e}^{I_{2}(t_{2})}=1
so that we can work directly with
R_{2}=\tilde{R}_{2}
). Using the bound for the evolution family in Lemma
6.1
and the bound for the residual in Lemma
6.2
, we obtain

\begin{split}\|R_{2}(\cdot,t_{2})\|_{H_{ul}^{\theta}}\leq&C\|R_{2}^{\ast}\|_{H_{ul}^{\theta}}+C\int_{0}^{t_{2}}\|F_{2}(\cdot,s_{2})\|_{H_{ul}^{\theta}}ds_{2}\\
&+Cr_{2}^{n-\beta-2}\int_{0}^{t_{2}}\left(\delta_{n,4}\max_{m\in I_{N}}|\nu_{m}|+\sup_{m,j}\|A_{mj,2}(\cdot,s_{2})\|_{H_{ul}^{\theta_{\alpha(m)+j,N}}}\right)ds_{2}.\end{split}

If we set
\beta=n-2
and use Lemma
6.4
together with the fact that each
A_{mj,2}(\cdot,s_{2})\in H_{ul}^{\theta_{\alpha(m)+j,N}}
for all
s_{2}\in[0,T_{2}]
(this follows from Lemma
5.16
), we obtain

\|R_{2}(\cdot,t_{2})\|_{H_{ul}^{\theta}}\leq C\left(1+\|R_{2}^{\ast}\|_{H_{ul}^{\theta}}\right)+C\tilde{C}(\tilde{M},\tilde{K})\left(1+r_{2}^{n-3}+r_{2}^{2(n-3)}\right)\int_{0}^{t_{2}}\|R_{2}(\cdot,s_{2})\|_{H_{ul}^{\theta}}ds_{2},

for all
t_{2}\in[0,\tilde{T}_{2}]
, where the constant
\tilde{C}(\tilde{M},\tilde{K})>0
can again be chosen as small as necessary by choosing sufficiently small
K,M>0
. Applying Grönwall’s inequality yields

\|R_{2}(\cdot,t_{2})\|_{H_{ul}^{\theta}}\leq C\left(1+\|R_{2}^{\ast}\|_{H_{ul}^{\theta}}\right)\exp\left(C\tilde{C}(\tilde{M},\tilde{K})\left(1+r_{2}^{n-3}+r_{2}^{2(n-3)}\right)t_{2}\right).

It follows that
R_{2}(\cdot,t_{2})\in H_{ul}^{\theta}
for all
t_{2}\in[0,T_{2}]
(recall that
T_{2}
is finite).

Similarly to the
\mathcal{K}_{1}
analysis above, the bound on the residual integral can be improved if
\mu(x)\equiv 0
because we can use the bound (
158
) in Lemma
6.2
. Using this result to improve the bound on the right-most integral in (
164
) leads to

\begin{split}\|R_{2}(\cdot,t_{2})\|_{H_{ul}^{\theta}}\leq C&\left(\|R_{2}^{\ast}\|_{H_{ul}^{\theta}}+\max_{k=1,\ldots,N-2}\|A_{k,2}^{\ast}\|_{H_{ul}^{\theta_{k,N-2}}}\right)+\\
&\qquad\qquad C\tilde{C}(\tilde{M},\tilde{K})\left(1+r_{2}^{n-3}+r_{2}^{2(n-3)}\right)\int_{0}^{t_{2}}\|R_{2}(\cdot,s_{2})\|_{H_{ul}^{\theta}}ds_{2},\end{split}

which holds for all
t_{2}\in[0,\tilde{T}_{2}]
. The bound in Assertion (ii) follows after an application of Grönwall’s inequality.

It remains to prove Assertion (iii). We work in chart
\mathcal{K}_{3}
, set
\mu(x)\equiv 0
and assume that the hypotheses of Proposition
5.25
are satisfied. We have

\begin{split}\tilde{R}_{3}(\cdot,t_{3})=\mathrm{e}^{t_{3}\Lambda_{3}}R_{3}^{\ast}&+\int_{0}^{t_{3}}\mathrm{e}^{(t_{3}-s_{3})\Lambda_{3}}F_{3}(\cdot,s_{3})ds_{3}\\
&+\int_{0}^{t_{3}}\mathrm{e}^{(t_{3}-s_{3})\Lambda_{3}}r_{3}(s_{3})^{-\beta-2}\mathrm{e}^{-I_{3}(s_{3})}\textup{Res}(r_{3}(s_{3})\psi_{3}(\cdot,s_{3}))ds_{3},\end{split}

for
t_{3}\in[0,\tilde{T}_{3}]
. Using the bound for the evolution family in Lemma
6.1
, we obtain

\begin{split}\|\tilde{R}_{3}(\cdot,t_{3})\|_{H_{ul}^{\theta}}\leq C\mathrm{e}^{c_{3}t_{3}}\|R_{3}^{\ast}\|_{H_{ul}^{\theta}}&+C\int_{0}^{t_{3}}\mathrm{e}^{c_{3}(t_{3}-s_{3})}\|F_{3}(\cdot,s_{3})\|_{H_{ul}^{\theta}}ds_{3}\\
+C\int_{0}^{t_{3}}\mathrm{e}^{c_{3}(t_{3}-s_{3})}&r_{3}(s_{3})^{-\beta-2}\mathrm{e}^{-I_{3}(s_{3})}\|\textup{Res}(r_{3}(s_{3})\psi_{3}(\cdot,s_{3}))\|_{H_{ul}^{\theta}}ds_{3},\end{split}

for all
t_{3}\in[0,\tilde{T}_{3}]
. The right-most integral can be bounded using Lemma
6.2
and the fact that
r_{3}(s_{1})^{-\beta-2}\mathrm{e}^{-I_{3}(s_{1})}=1/({r_{3}^{\ast}}^{\beta}r_{3}(s_{3})^{2})
. We obtain
